 [\lVert f\rVert_{\infty,2}\coloneqq\sup_{0\leqslant t\leqslant h}\,\lVert f(t)\rVert_{L^{2}}\,.] 



@S4.SS4.SSS3.p5.2 In particular, we can identify the position coordinates  [X] ,  [X^{[0]}] ,  [X^{[1]}]  of the Hamiltonian flow and Picard iterations, respectively, as well as  [g] , as  [L^{2}(\Pi_{\eta};\mathbb{R}^{d})] -valued curves.


@S4.SS4.SSS3.p6 

@S4.SS4.SSS3.p6.1 Apply the Banach-valued interpolation remainder in
Lemma B.2 to the curve
 [g:[0,h]\to L^{2}(\Pi_{\eta};\mathbb{R}^{d})] . Together with
(4.26), it gives





 [\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{J,2}] 
 [\displaystyle\coloneqq\sup_{0\leqslant t\leqslant h}\,\lVert g(t)-\mathcal{I}_{J,h}g(t)\rVert_{L^{2}}\leqslant\frac{h^{J}}{J!}\sup_{0\leqslant t\leqslant h}\,\lVert g^{(J)}(t)\rVert_{L^{2}}] 





 [\displaystyle\leqslant C^{J+1}\sqrt{J!}\,\sqrt{d}\,\Bigl(\frac{h}{\sqrt{\eta}}\Bigr)^{J}\leqslant B_{J,2}\sqrt{d}\,\Bigl(\frac{h}{\sqrt{\eta}}\Bigr)^{J}\,.] 



@S4.SS4.SSS3.p6.2 Since
the exact flow is stationary under  [\Pi_{\eta}] , integration by parts gives





 [\lVert g_{\eta}\circ X\rVert_{\infty,2}^{2}=\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{\eta}}\lVert g_{\eta}\rVert^{2}=\mathbb{E}_{\pi_{\eta}}\Delta V_{\eta}\leqslant d\,.] 



@S4.SS4.SSS3.p6.3 Therefore, the integral equation for  [X]  shows that





 [\lVert X^{[0]}-X\rVert_{\infty,2}\leqslant\sqrt{d}\,\frac{h^{2}}{2}\,.] 

(4.28)


@S4.SS4.SSS3.p6.4 To control the first Picard iteration, insert the interpolant of the force along
the exact trajectory:





 [\displaystyle X_{t}^{[1]}-X_{t}] 
 [\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t}(t-s)\,\bigl\{g_{\eta}(X_{s})-\mathcal{I}_{J,h}[g_{\eta}\circ X^{[0]}](s)\bigr\}\,\mathrm{d}s] 





 [\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t}(t-s)\,\bigl\{g_{\eta}(X_{s})-\mathcal{I}_{J,h}[g_{\eta}\circ X](s)\bigr\}\,\mathrm{d}s+\int_{0}^{t}(t-s)\,\mathcal{I}_{J,h}[g_{\eta}\circ X-g_{\eta}\circ X^{[0]}](s)\,\mathrm{d}s\,.] 



@S4.SS4.SSS3.p6.5 Because  [g_{\eta}]  is  [1] -Lipschitz,
(4.28) yields





 [\displaystyle\lVert X^{[1]}-X\rVert_{\infty,2}] 
 [\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{h^{2}}{2}\,\bigl\{\mathcal{E}_{J,2}+\Lambda_{J}\,\lVert X^{[0]}-X\rVert_{\infty,2}\bigr\}\leqslant\frac{\mathcal{E}_{J,2}}{2}\,h^{2}+\frac{\Lambda_{J}\sqrt{d}}{4}\,h^{4}\,.] 




@S4.SS4.SSS3.p7 

@S4.SS4.SSS3.p7.1 Set  [(\widehat{X}_{h},\widehat{P}_{h})\coloneqq(X_{h}^{[2]},P_{h}^{[2]})] , the
Hamiltonian endpoint produced by the exact gradient numerical scheme. For
the momentum endpoint, insert the interpolant of the force along the exact
trajectory once more to obtain





 [\displaystyle\lVert\widehat{P}_{h}-P_{h}\rVert_{L^{2}}\leqslant\mathcal{E}_{J,2}\,h+\Lambda_{J}h\,\lVert X^{[1]}-X\rVert_{\infty,2}] 
 [\displaystyle\leqslant\Bigl(1+\frac{h^{2}\Lambda_{J}}{2}\Bigr)\,h\,\mathcal{E}_{J,2}+\frac{\Lambda_{J}^{2}\sqrt{d}}{4}\,h^{5}] 





 [\displaystyle\leqslant C\mathcal{E}_{J,2}\,h+C\Lambda_{J}^{2}\sqrt{d}\,h^{5}\,.] 



@S4.SS4.SSS3.p7.2 Similarly,





 [\displaystyle\lVert\widehat{X}_{h}-X_{h}\rVert_{L^{2}}\leqslant\frac{h^{2}}{2}\,\mathcal{E}_{J,2}+\frac{\Lambda_{J}h^{2}}{2}\,\lVert X^{[1]}-X\rVert_{\infty,2}] 
 [\displaystyle\leqslant\Bigl(1+\frac{h^{2}\Lambda_{J}}{2}\Bigr)\,\frac{h^{2}}{2}\,\mathcal{E}_{J,2}+\frac{\Lambda_{J}^{2}\sqrt{d}}{8}\,h^{6}] 





 [\displaystyle\leqslant C\mathcal{E}_{J,2}\,h^{2}+C\Lambda_{J}^{2}\sqrt{d}\,h^{6}\,.] 



@S4.SS4.SSS3.p7.3 In the last lines, we used  [h^{2}\Lambda_{J}\leqslant 1] .


@S4.SS4.SSS3.p8 

@S4.SS4.SSS3.p8.1 Finally, start both the numerical phase and an exact Hamiltonian phase from
 [\Pi_{\eta}] , and use the same Gaussian variables in the two OU refreshes.
The first refresh preserves  [\Pi_{\eta}] , so the preceding stationary bounds
apply. The exact Hamiltonian flow and the final refresh also preserve
 [\Pi_{\eta}] . In the final refresh the common Gaussian increment cancels,
while the momentum difference is multiplied by  [e^{-h/2}] . Consequently,
using also  [h\leqslant 1] , which follows from  [h^{2}\Lambda_{J}\leqslant 1]  and
 [\Lambda_{J}\geqslant 1] ,





 [\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{{\rm disc}}] 
 [\displaystyle\leqslant\lVert\widehat{X}_{h}-X_{h}\rVert_{L^{2}}+\lVert\widehat{P}_{h}-P_{h}\rVert_{L^{2}}\leqslant C\mathcal{E}_{J,2}\,h+C\Lambda_{J}^{2}\sqrt{d}\,h^{5}\leqslant C\sqrt{d}\,\Bigl[B_{J,2}h\,\Bigl(\frac{h}{\sqrt{\eta}}\Bigr)^{J}+\Lambda_{J}^{2}h^{5}\Bigr]\,.] 



@S4.SS4.SSS3.p8.2 This proves (4.27).
∎





@S4.SS5 

4.5 Proof of
Theorem 1.1


@S4.SS5.p1 

@S4.SS5.p1.1 We record the following standard initialization lemma.


@S4.Thmtheorem16 
Lemma 4.16 (Initialization).

@S4.Thmtheorem16.p1 

@S4.Thmtheorem16.p1.1 Let  [\widehat{\mu}_{0}\coloneqq\delta_{x_{\rm ref}}\otimes\mathsf{N}(0,I)] . For every
 [q\geqslant 2] ,





 [W_{q,M_{\kappa}}(\widehat{\mu}_{0},\Pi_{\eta})\leqslant C\sqrt{\kappa\,(d+q)}\,.] 





@S4.SS5.2 
Proof.

@S4.SS5.p2 

@S4.SS5.p2.1 Let  [x_{\star}]  minimize  [V] . Strong monotonicity and the assumption
on  [\lVert\nabla V(x_{\rm ref})\rVert]  give





 [\lVert x_{\rm ref}-x_{\star}\rVert\leqslant\kappa\,\lVert\nabla V(x_{\rm ref})\rVert\leqslant\sqrt{\kappa d}\,.] 



@S4.SS5.p2.2 If  [X\sim\pi] , concentration from strong log-concavity
[6, Chapter 5] and integration by parts give
 [\lVert X-x_{\star}\rVert_{L^{q}}\leqslant C\sqrt{\kappa\,(d+q)}] . A draw from  [\pi_{\eta}]  is given by  [X+\sqrt{\eta}\,G] , and
 [\sqrt{\eta}\,\lVert G\rVert_{L^{q}}\leqslant C\sqrt{\kappa\,(d+q)}]  because
 [\eta\leqslant 1\leqslant\kappa] . Coupling the initial and stationary momenta
identically, the claim follows from the triangle inequality and  [M_{\kappa}\preceq CI] .
∎



@S4.SS5.p3 

@S4.SS5.p3.1 The following lemma handles the terminal step.


@S4.Thmtheorem17 
Lemma 4.17 (Terminal RGO step in  [W_{2}] ).

@S4.Thmtheorem17.p1 

@S4.Thmtheorem17.p1.1 Suppose  [\kappa^{-1}I\preceq\nabla^{2}V\preceq I] . For every
 [\eta>0] , write
 [\pi_{\eta}\coloneqq\pi*\mathsf{N}(0,\eta I)]  and
 [R_{\eta}(y,\mathrm{d}x)\coloneqq R_{\eta,y}(\mathrm{d}x)] . Then, for every
 [y,y^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}] ,





 [W_{2}(R_{\eta,y},R_{\eta,y^{\prime}})\leqslant\frac{\kappa}{\kappa+\eta}\,\lVert y-y^{\prime}\rVert\leqslant\lVert y-y^{\prime}\rVert\,.] 



@S4.Thmtheorem17.p1.2 Moreover,  [\pi_{\eta}R_{\eta}=\pi] . Consequently, for any center laws
 [\mu,\mu^{\prime}] ,





 [W_{2}(\mu R_{\eta},\mu^{\prime}R_{\eta})\leqslant W_{2}(\mu,\mu^{\prime})\,.] 





@S4.SS5.3 
Proof.

@S4.SS5.p4 

@S4.SS5.p4.1 These are the standard invariance and contraction properties of the
restricted Gaussian oracle; see [37, 19].
∎



@S4.SS5.p5 

@S4.SS5.p5.1 The proof of Theorem 1.1 is completed by
plugging the bias, variance, and
discretization error bounds from Section 4.4 into
(4.12) and performing some bookkeeping.


@S4.SS5.4 
Proof of Theorem 1.1.

@S4.SS5.p6 

@S4.SS5.p6.1 Throughout, we assume access to exact proximal queries; this assumption can be removed via the arguments in in Appendix D. After the change of variables  [z=\sqrt{\beta}\,x] , it suffices to work
under  [\beta=1]  and prove  [W_{2}\leqslant\sqrt{\kappa}\,\varepsilon] .
Choose





 [\displaystyle\mathfrak{L}] 
 [\displaystyle\coloneqq\log\frac{C\kappa d}{\varepsilon}\,,\qquad J\coloneqq\lceil C\mathfrak{L}^{2}\rceil\,,\qquad h\coloneqq\frac{c}{\mathfrak{L}}\min\Bigl\{\frac{1}{\kappa},\frac{\varepsilon^{1/3}}{\kappa^{1/6}d^{1/6}}\Bigr\}\,,\qquad N\coloneqq\Bigl\lceil\frac{C\kappa\mathfrak{L}}{h}\Bigr\rceil\,,] 



@S4.SS5.p6.2 where  [c,C>0]  are universal constants; the following assertions hold after adjusting these constants appropriately.
Here,  [\mathfrak{L}]  is introduced to streamline the bookkeeping of logarithmic terms.


@S4.SS5.p7 

@S4.SS5.p7.1 Let  [\theta>0] ,
to be chosen below, and choose  [\eta>0]  so that
 [h^{2}/\eta=\theta] .
The explicit choice of  [B_{J,2}]  in
Proposition 4.15 gives





 [\displaystyle B_{J,2}\theta^{J/2}] 
 [\displaystyle=C_{0}^{J+1}\sqrt{J!}\,\theta^{J/2}\leqslant C_{0}^{J+1}(J\theta)^{J/2}\leqslant e^{-c_{1}J}\leqslant h^{4}\,,] 

(4.29)


@S4.SS5.p7.2 provided that  [\theta\leqslant c\mathfrak{L}^{-2}] . We henceforth choose  [\theta\coloneqq c/\mathfrak{L}^{2}] .
Then,  [J\geqslant 2]  and
 [h\leqslant c/(\kappa\log J)] ,
so the restrictions in
Propositions 4.15 and 4.7
hold, and moreover  [\eta\leqslant 1] .


@S4.SS5.p8 

@S4.SS5.p8.1 Take square roots in (4.12) and substitute
the local bounds from
Propositions 4.13, 4.9 and 4.15.
Together with Lemma 4.16, this gives





 [\displaystyle W_{2,M_{\kappa}}(\widehat{\mu}_{N}^{\rm ph},\Pi_{\eta})\leqslant{}] 
 [\displaystyle C\sqrt{\kappa d}\,e^{-cNh/\kappa}+C\Lambda_{J}^{2}\sqrt{\kappa d}\,(\eta h+\eta^{1/2}h^{5/2})] 





 [\displaystyle\qquad{}+C\kappa\sqrt{d}\,(\eta^{3/2}+\Lambda_{J}\eta^{1/2}h^{2}+\Lambda_{J}^{2}h^{4})\,,] 



@S4.SS5.p8.2 where  [\widehat{\mu}_{N}^{\rm ph}]  is the final phase-space law. The Picard iteration error
term is included in the last monomial by
(4.29). Since
 [\Lambda_{J}\leqslant C\log\mathfrak{L}] , the preceding bounds on
 [h,\eta]  show that the sum of the last five terms can is at most  [\sqrt{\kappa}\,\varepsilon/4]  for  [c]  appropriately small.
This bookkeeping is uniform over  [0<\varepsilon\leqslant 1] . The definition of  [N]  makes the initial
term at most  [\sqrt{\kappa}\,\varepsilon/4] .


@S4.SS5.p9 

@S4.SS5.p9.1 By Lemma 4.17,





 [W_{2}(\widehat{\mu}_{N}R_{\eta},\pi)=W_{2}(\widehat{\mu}_{N}R_{\eta},\pi_{\eta}R_{\eta})\leqslant W_{2}(\widehat{\mu}_{N},\pi_{\eta})\leqslant CW_{2,M_{\kappa}}(\widehat{\mu}_{N}^{\rm ph},\Pi_{\eta})\leqslant\frac{\sqrt{\kappa}\,\varepsilon}{2}\,,] 



@S4.SS5.p9.2 after adjusting universal constants. Let  [\widehat{R}_{\eta,y}]  be the
conditional output law of the terminal FORS routine in
Algorithm 3.2, and set





 [\delta_{\rm term}\coloneqq\min\Bigl\{\frac{1}{2},\frac{\kappa\varepsilon^{2}}{8\eta}\Bigr\}\,.] 



@S4.SS5.p9.3 By Theorem A.4 with  [q=2]  and
 [\varepsilon^{2}\coloneqq\min\{1/4,\delta_{\rm term}\}] , uniformly in  [y] ,
we can ensure that





 [\KL(\widehat{R}_{\eta,y}\mathbin{\|}R_{\eta,y})\leqslant\mathsf{R}_{2}(\widehat{R}_{\eta,y}\mathbin{\|}R_{\eta,y})\leqslant\delta_{\rm term}\,.] 



@S4.SS5.p9.4 The RGO potential is
 [(\kappa^{-1}+\eta^{-1})] -strongly convex.
Talagrand’s inequality therefore gives





 [W_{2}^{2}(\widehat{R}_{\eta,y},R_{\eta,y})\leqslant\frac{2\delta_{\rm term}}{\kappa^{-1}+\eta^{-1}}\leqslant 2\eta\delta_{\rm term}\leqslant\frac{\kappa\varepsilon^{2}}{4}\,.] 



@S4.SS5.p9.5 The triangle inequality then yields
 [W_{2}(\widehat{\mu}_{N}\widehat{R}_{\eta},\pi)\leqslant\sqrt{\kappa}\,\varepsilon] .


@S4.SS5.p10 

@S4.SS5.p10.1 Each phase uses  [2J]  gradients and  [2J]  proximal calls. Substitution
of  [N]  gives





 [2JN\leqslant C\,\Bigl\{\kappa^{2}+\frac{\kappa^{7/6}d^{1/6}}{\varepsilon^{1/3}}\Bigr\}\,\mathfrak{L}^{4}] 



@S4.SS5.p10.2 calls of each type. By Theorem A.4, the terminal routine
has expected cost at most  [C\mathfrak{L}^{5/3}\,(1+\varepsilon^{4/9}d^{1/9}/\kappa^{2/9})] .
Any fixed power of
 [\mathfrak{L}]  is dominated by a positive power of
 [\kappa d/\varepsilon] . Hence this cost is dominated by the Picard
HMC bound for  [\kappa,d\geqslant 1]  and  [0<\varepsilon\leqslant 1] . This proves the theorem.
∎





@S5 

5  [W_{q}]  analysis


@S5.p1 

@S5.p1.1 For our warm start application, we need to upgrade the  [W_{2}]  guarantee to a  [W_{q}]  guarantee, for  [q\geqslant 2] .
We organize the proof in parallel with Section 4, and along the way, we describe the new ingredients needed for this part of the analysis.


@S5.SS1 

5.1  [W_{q}]  guarantee


@S5.Thmtheorem1 
Theorem 5.1 ( [W_{q}]  guarantee).

@S5.Thmtheorem1.p1 

@S5.Thmtheorem1.p1.1 Fix any  [c_{0}>0] . There is a constant  [C=C(c_{0})>0]  with the following
property. Suppose
 [\kappa\geqslant 1] ,  [V\in C^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})] , and





 [\kappa^{-1}I\preceq\nabla^{2}V\preceq I\,,\qquad\lVert\nabla V(x_{\rm ref})\rVert\leqslant\sqrt{d/\kappa}\,.] 



@S5.Thmtheorem1.p1.2 For every  [q\geqslant 2]  and  [0<\varepsilon\leqslant 1] , put





 [\mathfrak{L}_{q}\coloneqq q+\log\frac{e\kappa d}{\varepsilon}\,.] 



@S5.Thmtheorem1.p1.3 Then, there is a smoothing variance  [0<\eta\leqslant c_{0}]  for which a gradient-only
implementation of Picard HMC returns a sample whose output law  [\widehat{\pi}_{\eta}]  satisfies





 [W_{q}(\widehat{\pi}_{\eta},\pi_{\eta})\leqslant\varepsilon\,.] 



@S5.Thmtheorem1.p1.4 Its expected number of gradient queries is at most





 [C\,\Bigl\{\kappa^{2}+\frac{\kappa^{4/3}\,(d+q)^{1/6}}{\varepsilon^{1/3}}\Bigr\}\,\mathfrak{L}_{q}^{6}\,.] 





@S5.SS1.p1 

@S5.SS1.p1.1 Note that unlike Theorem 1.1, this guarantee targets the smoothed distribution  [\pi_{\eta}] ; that is, we omit the terminal FORS step.
This is because the terminal FORS guarantee does not immediately hold in  [W_{q}] ; moreover, the guarantee for sampling from  [\pi_{\eta}]  will be enough for our eventual warm start application.
Indeed, the guarantee is intended to implement the smooth sampler 
                    
                      A2
                    
                   in Section 6.



@S5.SS2 

5.2  [W_{q}]  local error analysis


@S5.SS2.p1 

@S5.SS2.p1.1 In the  [W_{2}]  proof, the local error framework (Lemma 4.4) separates a weak error from a
strong error by expanding a square. For  [q>2] , we replace
it with the following smoothness inequality.


@S5.Thmtheorem2 
Lemma 5.2 ( [L^{q}]  smoothness).

@S5.Thmtheorem2.p1 

@S5.Thmtheorem2.p1.1 Let  [q\geqslant 2] . If  [Z]  is a centered random vector in a Hilbert space and
 [a]  is deterministic, then





 [(\mathbb{E}\lVert a+Z\rVert^{q})^{2/q}\leqslant\lVert a\rVert^{2}+(q-1)\,(\mathbb{E}\lVert Z\rVert^{q})^{2/q}\,.] 

(5.1)




@S5.SS2.2 
Proof.

@S5.SS2.p2 

@S5.SS2.p2.1 This follows from the smoothness of  [L^{q}] ; see [53]. It also
follows directly by differentiating
 [t\mapsto(\mathbb{E}\lVert a+tZ\rVert^{q})^{2/q}] : centeredness gives zero derivative at
the origin and Hölder’s inequality bounds the second derivative by
 [2\,(q-1)\,\lVert Z\rVert_{L^{q}}^{2}] .
∎



@S5.Thmtheorem3 
Lemma 5.3 (Local error framework in  [W_{q}] ).

@S5.Thmtheorem3.p1 

@S5.Thmtheorem3.p1.1 Let  [P,\widehat{P}]  be Markov kernels on  [\mathbb{R}^{m}] , and let  [q\geqslant 2] .
Suppose that, for every  [x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{m}] , there is a coupling
 [X\sim\delta_{x}P] ,  [Y\sim\delta_{y}P]  satisfying





 [\lVert\mathbb{E}(X-Y)\rVert\leqslant\rho\,\lVert x-y\rVert\,,\qquad\lVert(X-Y)-\mathbb{E}(X-Y)\rVert_{L^{q}}\leqslant\chi_{q}\,\lVert x-y\rVert\,.] 



@S5.Thmtheorem3.p1.2 Suppose also that, writing  [\widehat{X}\sim\delta_{x}\widehat{P}]  and
 [X\sim\delta_{x}P] ,





 [W_{q}(\delta_{x}\widehat{P},\delta_{x}P)\leqslant\mathcal{E}_{\rm strong}\,,\qquad\lVert\mathbb{E}\widehat{X}-\mathbb{E}X\rVert\leqslant\mathcal{E}_{\rm weak}\,.] 



@S5.Thmtheorem3.p1.3 Assume  [0<\rho<1]  and  [4\,(q-1)\,\chi_{q}^{2}\leqslant\rho\,(1-\rho)] .
Then, for all probability laws  [\mu,\nu]  on  [\mathbb{R}^{m}] ,





 [W_{q}^{2}(\mu\widehat{P},\nu P)\leqslant\rho W_{q}^{2}(\mu,\nu)+\frac{16\,(q-1)}{1+\rho}\,\mathcal{E}_{\rm strong}^{2}+\frac{2}{1-\rho}\,\mathcal{E}_{\rm weak}^{2}\,.] 





@S5.SS2.3 
Proof.

@S5.SS2.p3 

@S5.SS2.p3.1 Let  [(Z,Z^{\prime})]  be an optimal  [W_{q}]  coupling of  [\mu]  and  [\nu] . Given
 [(Z,Z^{\prime})] , let  [\widehat{X}\sim\widehat{P}(Z,\cdot)]  and  [X\sim P(Z,\cdot)]  be optimally coupled in  [W_{q}] , and let  [Y\sim P(Z^{\prime},\cdot)]  be coupled to  [X]  via the coupling in the theorem statement.
Write  [\Delta\coloneqq\widehat{X}-X] .
Conditionally on  [(Z,Z^{\prime})] , Jensen’s inequality gives





 [\lVert\mathbb{E}[\Delta\mid Z,Z^{\prime}]\rVert\leqslant\mathcal{E}_{\rm weak}\,,\qquad\lVert\Delta-\mathbb{E}[\Delta\mid Z,Z^{\prime}]\rVert_{L^{q}\mid Z,Z^{\prime}}\leqslant 2\mathcal{E}_{\rm strong}\,.] 



@S5.SS2.p3.2 By Minkowski’s inequality and Young’s inequality, for  [\lambda>0] ,





 [\displaystyle\lVert\widehat{X}-Y\rVert_{L^{q}\mid Z,Z^{\prime}}^{2}] 
 [\displaystyle\leqslant(1+\lambda)\,\lVert\Delta-\mathbb{E}[\Delta\mid Z,Z^{\prime}]+X-Y\rVert_{L^{q}\mid Z,Z^{\prime}}^{2}+(1+\lambda^{-1})\,\lVert\mathbb{E}[\Delta\mid Z,Z^{\prime}]\rVert_{L^{q}\mid Z,Z^{\prime}}^{2}\,.] 



@S5.SS2.p3.3 For the first term, by (5.1),






 [\displaystyle\lVert\Delta-\mathbb{E}[\Delta\mid Z,Z^{\prime}]+X-Y\rVert_{L^{q}\mid Z,Z^{\prime}}^{2}] 





 [\displaystyle\qquad\leqslant\lVert\mathbb{E}[X-Y\mid Z,Z^{\prime}]\rVert^{2}+(q-1)\,\lVert\Delta-\mathbb{E}[\Delta\mid Z,Z^{\prime}]+X-Y-\mathbb{E}[X-Y\mid Z,Z^{\prime}]\rVert_{L^{q}\mid Z,Z^{\prime}}^{2}] 





 [\displaystyle\qquad\leqslant\rho^{2}\,\lVert Z-Z^{\prime}\rVert^{2}+(q-1)\,\bigl(2\mathcal{E}_{\rm strong}+\chi_{q}\,\lVert Z-Z^{\prime}\rVert\bigr)^{2}\,.] 



@S5.SS2.p3.4 Using  [(u+v)^{2}\leqslant 2u^{2}+2v^{2}]  and the assumption on  [\chi_{q}] , the
right-hand side is at most





 [\frac{\rho\,(1+\rho)}{2}\,\lVert Z-Z^{\prime}\rVert^{2}+8\,(q-1)\,\mathcal{E}_{\rm strong}^{2}\,.] 



@S5.SS2.p3.5 Take the  [L^{q/2}]  norm over  [(Z,Z^{\prime})] .
Then, choosing  [\lambda=\frac{1-\rho}{1+\rho}] ,





 [\displaystyle\lVert\widehat{X}-Y\rVert_{L^{q}}^{2}] 
 [\displaystyle=\bigl\lVert\lVert\widehat{X}-Y\rVert_{L^{q}\mid Z,Z^{\prime}}^{2}\bigr\rVert_{L^{q/2}}] 





 [\displaystyle\leqslant\Bigl\lVert(1+\lambda)\,\Bigl(\frac{\rho\,(1+\rho)}{2}\,\lVert Z-Z^{\prime}\rVert^{2}+8\,(q-1)\,\mathcal{E}_{\rm strong}^{2}\Bigr)+(1+\lambda^{-1})\,\mathcal{E}_{\rm weak}^{2}\Bigr\rVert_{L^{q/2}}] 





 [\displaystyle=\rho\,\lVert Z-Z^{\prime}\rVert_{L^{q}}^{2}+\frac{16\,(q-1)}{1+\rho}\,\mathcal{E}_{\rm strong}^{2}+\frac{2}{1-\rho}\,\mathcal{E}_{\rm weak}^{2}\,.\qed] 





@S5.SS2.p4 

@S5.SS2.p4.1 Recall the kernels
 [\widehat{K}] ,  [\bar{K}] , and  [K]  from Section 4.1. For
 [q\geqslant 2] , define the centered variance and bias errors





 [\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{{\rm var},q}] 
 [\displaystyle\coloneqq\sup_{z\in\mathbb{R}^{2d}}W_{q}(\delta_{z}\widehat{K},\delta_{z}\bar{K})\,,] 
 [\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{{\rm bias}}] 
 [\displaystyle\coloneqq\sup_{z,\zeta\in\mathbb{R}^{2d}}\,\lVert\bar{\Phi}_{z}(\zeta)-\Phi_{z}(\zeta)\rVert\,.] 



@S5.SS2.p4.2 Take  [\widehat{P}\coloneqq\widehat{K}]  and  [P\coloneqq K] . By the definition of
 [\bar{\Phi}_{z}] , the laws  [\delta_{z}\widehat{K}]  and
 [\delta_{z}\bar{K}]  have the same mean. The triangle inequality yields





 [\mathcal{E}_{\rm strong}\leqslant\mathcal{E}_{{\rm var},q}+\mathcal{E}_{{\rm bias}}\,,\qquad\mathcal{E}_{\rm weak}\leqslant\mathcal{E}_{{\rm bias}}\,.] 



@S5.SS2.p4.3 Also, define





 [\mathcal{E}_{{\rm disc},q}\coloneqq W_{q}(\Pi_{\eta}K,\Pi_{\eta})\,.] 



@S5.SS2.p4.4 The analogue of Theorem 4.5 is the
following theorem.


@S5.Thmtheorem4 
Theorem 5.4 (Local error analysis in  [W_{q}] ).

@S5.Thmtheorem4.p1 

@S5.Thmtheorem4.p1.1 Let  [J\geqslant 2] , and assume  [h\leqslant c/(\kappa\,(q+\log J))] .
Then, for every phase-space law  [\widehat{\mu}] ,





 [W_{q,M_{\kappa}}^{2}(\widehat{\mu}\widehat{K},\Pi_{\eta})\leqslant(1-ch/\kappa)\,W_{q,M_{\kappa}}^{2}(\widehat{\mu},\Pi_{\eta})+Cq\,\mathcal{E}_{{\rm var},q}^{2}+\frac{C\kappa}{h}\,(\mathcal{E}_{{\rm bias}}^{2}+\mathcal{E}_{{\rm disc},q}^{2})\,.] 

(5.2)


@S5.Thmtheorem4.p1.2 Consequently,





 [\displaystyle W_{q,M_{\kappa}}^{2}(\widehat{\mu}\widehat{K}^{N},\Pi_{\eta})] 
 [\displaystyle\leqslant e^{-cNh/\kappa}\,W_{q,M_{\kappa}}^{2}(\widehat{\mu},\Pi_{\eta})+\frac{C\kappa q}{h}\,\mathcal{E}_{{\rm var},q}^{2}+\frac{C\kappa^{2}}{h^{2}}\,(\mathcal{E}_{{\rm bias}}^{2}+\mathcal{E}_{{\rm disc},q}^{2})\,.] 

(5.3)




@S5.SS2.4 
Proof.

@S5.SS2.p5 

@S5.SS2.p5.1 Repeat the proof of Theorem 4.5, using
Lemma 5.3 in place of the  [W_{2}] 
local error framework; Propositions 4.7 and 4.8
still apply and give  [\rho=1-ch/\kappa]  and  [\chi_{q}\leqslant 2Ch] .
∎




@S5.SS3 

5.3  [W_{q}]  local error bounds


@S5.SS3.p1 

@S5.SS3.p1.1 The bias term  [\mathcal{E}_{{\rm bias}}]  is the same as before, and is controlled by Proposition 4.9.
Thus, we focus on controlling the other two terms.


@S5.SS3.SSS1 

5.3.1 Variance bound


@S5.SS3.SSS1.p1 

@S5.SS3.SSS1.p1.1 For the variance bound, we must extend the second-order  [W_{2}]  estimate in Lemma 4.11 to  [W_{q}] .


@S5.Thmtheorem5 
Lemma 5.5 (Second-order  [W_{q}]  estimate).

@S5.Thmtheorem5.p1 

@S5.Thmtheorem5.p1.1 In the setting of Lemma 4.11, for all  [q\geqslant 2] , it holds that





 [W_{q}\bigl(\law\{L(Z+f(Z,G))\}\,,\law\{L(Z+\bar{f}(Z))\}\bigr)\leqslant C\sqrt{m}\,\bigl(\sqrt{q}\,\lVert L\Pi_{E}\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}\,B^{2}+q^{2}\,\lVert L\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}\,AB\bigr)\,.] 

(5.4)




@S5.SS3.SSS1.p2 

@S5.SS3.SSS1.p2.1 The proof, which is given in Section C.3, relies on the same strategy as Lemma 4.11, but relies on a more involved Gaussian divergence inequality (Lemma C.3).
Using this, we can control the variance term in  [W_{q}] .


@S5.Thmtheorem6 
Proposition 5.6 (Variance bound in  [W_{q}] ).

@S5.Thmtheorem6.p1 

@S5.Thmtheorem6.p1.1 For every  [q\geqslant 2] ,





 [\mathcal{E}_{{\rm var},q}\leqslant C\Lambda_{J}^{2}\sqrt{d}\,\bigl(\eta h^{3/2}q^{1/2}+\eta^{1/2}h^{3}q^{2}\bigr)\,.] 





@S5.SS3.SSS1.2 
Proof.

@S5.SS3.SSS1.p3 

@S5.SS3.SSS1.p3.1 Apply Lemma 5.5 with the constants in
Lemma 4.10.
∎




@S5.SS3.SSS2 

5.3.2 Discretization error bound


@S5.SS3.SSS2.p1 

@S5.SS3.SSS2.p1.1 Next, we need to replace the  [L^{2}]  bound (4.22) with an  [L^{q}]  version.
The following lemma reduces this to an  [L^{q}]  bound for the Gaussian divergence operator, which in turn is handled in Lemma C.3.


@S5.Thmtheorem7 
Lemma 5.7 (Divergence bound).

@S5.Thmtheorem7.p1 

@S5.Thmtheorem7.p1.1 Let  [X\sim\pi] ,  [G\sim\mathsf{N}(0,I)] , and
 [P\sim\mathsf{N}(0,I)]  be independent, and set  [Y\coloneqq X+\sqrt{\eta}\,G] . For a smooth
tensor-valued position vector field  [W=(W_{i})_{i\in[d]}] , we have the representation





 [\delta_{\pi_{\eta}}W(Y,P)=\mathbb{E}\bigl[\eta^{-1/2}\,\delta_{\gamma}\{W(X+\sqrt{\eta}\,\cdot,P)\}(G)\bigm|Y,P\bigr]\,.] 

(5.5)


@S5.Thmtheorem7.p1.2 Consequently, for every  [q\geqslant 2] ,





 [\lVert\delta_{\pi_{\eta}}W\rVert_{L^{q}(\Pi_{\eta})}\leqslant C\sqrt{q/\eta}\,\lVert W\rVert_{L^{q}(\Pi_{\eta};\mathrm{HS})}+Cq^{2}\,\lVert DW\rVert_{L^{q}(\Pi_{\eta};\mathrm{HS})}\,.] 

(5.6)


@S5.Thmtheorem7.p1.3 The constant is independent of all tensor dimensions.



@S5.SS3.SSS2.2 
Proof.

@S5.SS3.SSS2.p2 

@S5.SS3.SSS2.p2.1 The identity in (4.1) is equivalent to  [g_{\eta}(Y)=\eta^{-1/2}\,\mathbb{E}[G\mid Y]] .
The Gaussian divergence gives





 [\displaystyle\delta_{\gamma}\{W(X+\sqrt{\eta}\,\cdot,P)\}(G)] 
 [\displaystyle=\langle G,W(Y,P)\rangle-\eta^{1/2}\operatorname{div}_{x}W(Y,P)\,.] 



@S5.SS3.SSS2.p2.2 Here  [\operatorname{div}_{x}W\coloneqq\sum_{i\in[d]}\partial_{x_{i}}W_{i}]  is the divergence in the position argument; the  [\eta^{1/2}]  factor comes from the chain rule.
Conditioning on  [(Y,P)]  proves
(5.5). Conditional expectation is an
 [L^{q}]  contraction. Apply Jensen’s inequality and (C.15) to obtain (5.6).
∎



@S5.Thmtheorem8 
Lemma 5.8 (Stationary iterated derivatives in  [L^{q}] ).

@S5.Thmtheorem8.p1 

@S5.Thmtheorem8.p1.1 For  [q\geqslant 2]  and  [k\geqslant 0] ,





 [\lVert\mathcal{L}_{\mathsf{H}}^{k}g_{\eta}\rVert_{L^{q}(\Pi_{\eta})}\leqslant C^{k+1}q^{2k}\,(k!)^{2}\,\sqrt{d+q}\,\eta^{-k/2}\,.] 

(5.7)




@S5.SS3.SSS2.3 
Proof.

@S5.SS3.SSS2.p3 

@S5.SS3.SSS2.p3.1 For a tensor-valued  [F] , define the weighted Sobolev norm





 [\lVert F\rVert_{\mathsf{W}^{N,q}}\coloneqq\sum_{j=0}^{N}\frac{\eta^{j/2}}{j!}\,\lVert D_{x}^{j}F\rVert_{L^{q}(\Pi_{\eta};\mathrm{HS})}\,.] 

(5.8)


@S5.SS3.SSS2.p3.2 Tracing through the proof of Lemma 4.14, but using (5.6) in place of (4.22), we obtain





 [\lVert\delta_{\pi_{\eta}}W\rVert_{\mathsf{W}^{N-1,q}}\leqslant\frac{Cq^{2}N}{\sqrt{\eta}}\,\lVert W\rVert_{\mathsf{W}^{N,q}}\,.] 

(5.9)


@S5.SS3.SSS2.p3.3 Recall the factorization  [\mathcal{L}_{\mathsf{H}}F=\delta_{\gamma}(D_{x}F)-\delta_{\pi_{\eta}}(D_{p}F)]  from (4.24). If  [F]  is polynomial of
degree at most  [r]  in  [p] , then
Lemma C.8, (5.8), and
(5.9) yield the
dimension-free bound





 [\lVert\mathcal{L}_{\mathsf{H}}F\rVert_{\mathsf{W}^{N-1,q}}\leqslant\frac{CN\,(\sqrt{q}+q^{2}r)}{\sqrt{\eta}}\,\lVert F\rVert_{\mathsf{W}^{N,q}}\,.] 

(5.10)



@S5.SS3.SSS2.p4 

@S5.SS3.SSS2.p4.1 It remains to initialize the induction.
From [24, Lemma 6.2.7], we have  [\lVert g_{\eta}\rVert_{L^{q}(\pi_{\eta})}\leqslant C\sqrt{d+q}] . For  [j\geqslant 1] , the uniform
bound (4.7) and the weights in
(5.8) give





 [\lVert g_{\eta}\rVert_{\mathsf{W}^{N,q}}\leqslant C(N+1)\sqrt{d+q}\,.] 



@S5.SS3.SSS2.p4.2 Finally,  [\mathcal{L}_{\mathsf{H}}^{j}g_{\eta}]  is polynomial of degree at most  [j]  in
momentum. Apply (5.10)  [k]  times, starting at  [N=k]  and
using  [r=j]  at step  [j] . Since
 [\sqrt{q}+q^{2}j\leqslant q^{2}\,(j+1)]  for  [q\geqslant 2] , the product of the
one-step factors is bounded by





 [\prod_{j=0}^{k-1}[(k-j)\,(\sqrt{q}+q^{2}j)]\leqslant q^{2k}\,(k!)^{2}\,.] 



@S5.SS3.SSS2.p4.3 For  [k\geqslant 1] , the initialization factor satisfies  [k+1\leqslant 2^{k}]  and
can therefore be absorbed into  [C^{k+1}] ; the case  [k=0]  follows
directly from the initialization bound. This proves
(5.7).
∎



@S5.Thmtheorem9 
Proposition 5.9 (Discretization error bound).

@S5.Thmtheorem9.p1 

@S5.Thmtheorem9.p1.1 If  [h^{2}\Lambda_{J}\leqslant 1] , then, for every  [q\geqslant 2] ,





 [\mathcal{E}_{{\rm disc},q}\leqslant C\sqrt{d+q}\,\Bigl[B_{J,q}h\,\Bigl(\frac{h}{\sqrt{\eta}}\Bigr)^{J}+\Lambda_{J}^{2}h^{5}\Bigr]\,.] 



@S5.Thmtheorem9.p1.2 Here, one may take
 [B_{J,q}=C_{0}^{J+1}q^{2J}J!] , where  [C_{0}]  is universal.



@S5.SS3.SSS2.4 
Proof.

@S5.SS3.SSS2.p5 

@S5.SS3.SSS2.p5.1 Repeat the proof of Proposition 4.15 with
the uniform-in-time  [L^{q}]  norm in place of the uniform-in-time  [L^{2}] 
norm. The interpolation estimate is replaced by





 [\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{J,q}] 
 [\displaystyle\coloneqq\sup_{0\leqslant t\leqslant h}\,\lVert g(t)-\mathcal{I}_{J,h}g(t)\rVert_{L^{q}(\Pi_{\eta})}\leqslant\frac{h^{J}}{J!}\sup_{0\leqslant t\leqslant h}\,\lVert g^{(J)}(t)\rVert_{L^{q}(\Pi_{\eta})}\leqslant C^{J+1}q^{2J}J!\sqrt{d+q}\,\Bigl(\frac{h}{\sqrt{\eta}}\Bigr)^{J}\,.] 

(5.11)


@S5.SS3.SSS2.p5.2 Also, stationarity and [24, Lemma 6.2.7] give





 [\sup_{0\leqslant t\leqslant h}\,\lVert g_{\eta}(X_{t})\rVert_{L^{q}(\Pi_{\eta})}=\lVert g_{\eta}\rVert_{L^{q}(\pi_{\eta})}\leqslant C\sqrt{d+q}] 



@S5.SS3.SSS2.p5.3 in place of the corresponding  [L^{2}]  bound by  [\sqrt{d}] .
∎





@S5.SS4 

5.4 Proof of
Theorem 5.1


@S5.SS4.2 
Proof of Theorem 5.1.

@S5.SS4.p1 

@S5.SS4.p1.1 We first assume exact proximal queries. The modifications needed to remove
proximal access are deferred to Appendix D.


@S5.SS4.p2 

@S5.SS4.p2.1 Fix  [q\geqslant 2]  and  [0<\varepsilon\leqslant 1] , and let  [\mathfrak{L}_{q}]  be as in
the theorem. Choose





 [h\coloneqq c\mathfrak{L}_{q}^{-3}\min\Bigl\{\frac{1}{\kappa},\Bigl(\frac{\varepsilon}{\kappa\sqrt{d+q}}\Bigr)^{1/3}\Bigr\}\,,\qquad\eta\coloneqq C_{\eta}\mathfrak{L}_{q}^{6}h^{2}\,,] 



@S5.SS4.p2.2 where  [C_{\eta}\geqslant 1]  is universal and  [c>0]  will be chosen
sufficiently small. Set





 [J\coloneqq\lceil C\mathfrak{L}_{q}\rceil\,,\qquad N\coloneqq\lceil C\kappa\mathfrak{L}_{q}/h\rceil\,.] 

(5.12)


@S5.SS4.p2.3 With appropriately large  [C]  and small  [c] , the definitions
give





 [e^{-C\mathfrak{L}_{q}}\leqslant h\leqslant\frac{c}{\kappa\mathfrak{L}_{q}^{3}}\,,\qquad h\mathfrak{L}_{q}\leqslant\frac{c}{\kappa\mathfrak{L}_{q}^{2}}\leqslant\frac{c}{\kappa}\,,\qquad\eta\leqslant\frac{C_{\eta}c^{2}}{\kappa^{2}}\leqslant\min\{c_{0},1/2\}\,,] 



@S5.SS4.p2.4 and  [\log(h^{-1})\leqslant C\mathfrak{L}_{q}] .
Since
 [B_{J,q}=C_{0}^{J+1}q^{2J}J!] , we have





 [B_{J,q}C_{\eta}^{-J/2}\mathfrak{L}_{q}^{-3J}\leqslant e^{-cJ}\,.] 



@S5.SS4.p2.5 A sufficiently large  [C]  therefore gives





 [B_{J,q}\,\Bigl(\frac{h}{\sqrt{\eta}}\Bigr)^{J}\leqslant h^{4}\,.] 



@S5.SS4.p2.6 Tedious bookkeeping shows that





 [W_{q,M_{\kappa}}(\widehat{\mu}_{N},\Pi_{\eta})\leqslant C\mathfrak{L}_{q}^{9}\kappa\sqrt{d+q}\,h^{3}\,,] 

(5.13)


@S5.SS4.p2.7 Each phase makes  [2J]  gradient queries and
 [2J]  exact proximal queries. Since
 [J\leqslant C\mathfrak{L}_{q}]  and
 [N\leqslant C\kappa\mathfrak{L}_{q}/h+1\leqslant C^{\prime}\kappa\mathfrak{L}_{q}/h] , the numbers of direct gradient queries
and exact proximal queries are each at most
 [C\kappa\mathfrak{L}_{q}^{2}/h] . Taking position marginals in
(5.13) and
the definition of  [h]  gives





 [W_{q}(\widehat{\pi}_{\eta},\pi_{\eta})\leqslant C\mathfrak{L}_{q}^{9}\kappa\sqrt{d+q}\,h^{3}\leqslant\varepsilon\,.] 



@S5.SS4.p2.8 The modifications in Appendix D remove the proximal
queries altogether with expected gradient cost at most
 [C\kappa\mathfrak{L}_{q}^{3}/h] . Substitution of  [h]  gives the query
bound in the theorem.
∎





@S6 

6 Recursive warm start generator


@S6.p1 

@S6.p1.1 In this section, we describe the recursive warm start generator and its guarantee.
See Section 3.4 for the algorithm and an overview.


@S6.SS1 

6.1 Main result


@S6.SS1.p1 

@S6.SS1.p1.1 For probability laws  [P,Q]  on  [\mathbb{R}^{d}] , define the sub-Gaussian
Orlicz–Wasserstein distance





 [W_{\psi_{2}}(P,Q)\coloneqq\inf_{(X,Y)}\inf\Bigl\{\varepsilon>0:\mathbb{E}\exp\Bigl(\frac{\lVert X-Y\rVert^{2}}{\varepsilon^{2}}\Bigr)\leqslant 2\Bigr\}\,,] 



@S6.SS1.p1.2 where the outer infimum ranges over all couplings of  [P]  and  [Q] .


@S6.SS1.p2 

@S6.SS1.p2.1 The recursive warm start generator assumes access to a smoothed sampler as follows.


@Thmassumptionx1 
Assumption (Smoothed sampler).

@Thmassumptionx1.p1 

@Thmassumptionx1.p1.1 There is a sampler  [\mathfrak{A}]  which, provided any normalized potential  [U]  satisfying  [\kappa_{U}^{-1}I\preceq\nabla^{2}U\preceq I] ,
and an admissible reference point  [x_{\rm ref}]  with  [\lVert\nabla U(x_{\rm ref})\rVert\leqslant\sqrt{d/\kappa_{U}}] , implements one of the interfaces below. Here,
 [\pi_{U,\eta}\coloneqq\pi_{U}*\mathsf{N}(0,\eta I)]  denotes the smoothed distribution.

 [\mathsf{A1}]  

@S6.I1.i1.p1 

@S6.I1.i1.p1.1 Given  [\varepsilon>0] , the sampler returns
 [0<\eta\leqslant c_{0}]  and a sample
with law  [\widehat{\pi}_{U,\eta}]  such that





 [\kappa_{U}^{-1/2}\,W_{2}(\widehat{\pi}_{U,\eta},\pi_{U,\eta})\leqslant\varepsilon] 



@S6.I1.i1.p1.2 using at most  [\mathsf{C}_{2}(\kappa_{U},d,\varepsilon)]  expected gradient queries.

 [\mathsf{A2}]  

@S6.I1.i2.p1 

@S6.I1.i2.p1.1 Given  [p\geqslant 2]  and  [\varepsilon>0] , it returns  [0<\eta\leqslant c_{0}]  and a
sample with law  [\widehat{\pi}_{U,\eta}]  such that





 [\kappa_{U}^{-1/2}\,W_{p}(\widehat{\pi}_{U,\eta},\pi_{U,\eta})\leqslant\varepsilon] 



@S6.I1.i2.p1.2 using at most  [\mathsf{C}_{p}(\kappa_{U},d,\varepsilon)]  expected gradient queries.

 [\mathsf{A3}]  

@S6.I1.i3.p1 

@S6.I1.i3.p1.1 Given  [\varepsilon>0] , it returns  [0<\eta\leqslant c_{0}]  and a sample with law
 [\widehat{\pi}_{U,\eta}]  such that





 [\kappa_{U}^{-1/2}\,W_{\psi_{2}}(\widehat{\pi}_{U,\eta},\pi_{U,\eta})\leqslant\varepsilon] 



@S6.I1.i3.p1.2 using at most  [\mathsf{C}_{\psi_{2}}(\kappa_{U},d,\varepsilon)]  expected gradient
queries.



@Thmassumptionx1.p1.2 Assume that each complexity function is non-decreasing in its condition number
argument and non-increasing in its accuracy argument, and that
 [p\mapsto\mathsf{C}_{p}]  is non-decreasing.



@S6.SS1.p3 

@S6.SS1.p3.1 We will apply this interface directly to potentials whose smoothness is not normalized to
 [1] . Concretely, if
 [\alpha_{U}I\preceq\nabla^{2}U\preceq\beta_{U}I] , apply  [\mathfrak{A}]  to
the normalized potential  [U(\cdot/\sqrt{\beta_{U}})] , and then divide
its output by  [\sqrt{\beta_{U}}] . If  [\eta]  is the normalized smoothing level, then the law of the rescaled output
approximates  [\pi_{U}*\mathsf{N}(0,\beta_{U}^{-1}\eta I)] .
The corresponding scale-invariant guarantee is
 [\sqrt{\alpha_{U}}\,W_{\bullet}\leqslant\varepsilon] , and the query cost is unchanged.


@S6.SS1.p4 

@S6.SS1.p4.1 In Sections 4 and 5, we have shown that Picard HMC implements the first two interfaces above.
We have not shown that it implements the third, but we include this case for completeness because, as shown below, 
                    
                      A3
                    
                   leads to a genuine Rényi warm start.


@S6.SS1.p5 

@S6.SS1.p5.1 We now present the main guarantee.


@S6.Thmtheorem1 
Theorem 6.1 (Recursive warm start generator).

@S6.Thmtheorem1.p1 

@S6.Thmtheorem1.p1.1 Suppose that  [\pi\propto\exp(-V)]  is  [\beta] -log-smooth, in the
sense that  [\nabla V]  is  [\beta] -Lipschitz, and satisfies the
logarithmic Sobolev inequality with constant  [\alpha^{-1}] .
Write  [\kappa\coloneqq\beta/\alpha] , and
suppose that we are given a point  [x_{\rm ref}]  satisfying  [V(x_{\rm ref})-\inf V\leqslant d] .


@S6.Thmtheorem1.p2 

@S6.Thmtheorem1.p2.1 Let the smoothed sampler  [\mathfrak{A}]  satisfy one of

                    
                      A1
                    
                  , 
                    
                      A2
                    
                  , and

                    
                      A3
                    
                  . Fix  [0<\delta,\Delta\leqslant 1/2] . For
 [q\geqslant 2] , write





 [\mathfrak{L}_{q}\coloneqq q+\log\frac{d\kappa q}{\Delta}\,.] 



@S6.Thmtheorem1.p2.2 Then the proximal sampler wrapper in Algorithm 3.4, with
each inner RGO sampled by Algorithm 3.3, has the
following guarantees.

(i) 

@S6.I2.i1.p1 

@S6.I2.i1.p1.1 Under 
                    
                      A1
                    
                  , the generator returns a
sample with law  [\widehat{\pi}]  satisfying





 [\KL(\widehat{\pi}\mathbin{\|}\pi)\leqslant\Delta^{2}\,.] 



@S6.I2.i1.p1.2 The expected total number of gradient queries is at most





 [C\kappa\mathfrak{L}_{2}^{2}\,\mathsf{C}_{2}\Bigl(6,d,\frac{c\Delta}{\sqrt{\kappa\mathfrak{L}_{2}}}\Bigr)+C\kappa\mathfrak{L}_{2}^{2}\,.] 



(ii) 

@S6.I2.i2.p1 

@S6.I2.i2.p1.1 Fix  [2\leqslant q<\infty] . Under 
                    
                      A2
                    
                  , the generator
returns a sample with law  [\widehat{\pi}]  for which there exists a law
 [\widehat{\pi}^{\dagger}]  satisfying





 [\mathsf{TV}(\widehat{\pi},\widehat{\pi}^{\dagger})\leqslant\delta\,,\qquad\mathsf{R}_{q}(\widehat{\pi}^{\dagger}\mathbin{\|}\pi)\leqslant\Delta^{2}\,.] 



@S6.I2.i2.p1.2 The expected total number of gradient queries is at most





 [C\kappa\mathfrak{L}_{q}^{2}\,\mathsf{C}_{p}\Bigl(6,d,\frac{c\Delta}{\sqrt{q\kappa\log(eq)\,\mathfrak{L}_{q}}}\Bigr)+C\kappa\mathfrak{L}_{q}\,\Bigl(\mathfrak{L}_{q}+\log\frac{C\kappa\mathfrak{L}_{q}}{\delta}\Bigr)\,,] 



@S6.I2.i2.p1.3 where





 [p\coloneqq 2\vee\Bigl\lceil\log\frac{C\kappa\mathfrak{L}_{q}^{2}}{\delta}\Bigr\rceil\,.] 



(iii) 

@S6.I2.i3.p1 

@S6.I2.i3.p1.1 Fix  [2\leqslant q<\infty] . Under 
                    
                      A3
                    
                  , the generator returns
a sample with law  [\widehat{\pi}]  satisfying





 [\mathsf{R}_{q}(\widehat{\pi}\mathbin{\|}\pi)\leqslant\Delta^{2}\,.] 



@S6.I2.i3.p1.2 The expected total number of gradient queries is at most





 [C\kappa\mathfrak{L}_{q}^{2}\,\mathsf{C}_{\psi_{2}}\Bigl(6,d,c\min\Bigl\{\frac{1}{q},\frac{\Delta}{\sqrt{q\kappa\log(eq)\,\mathfrak{L}_{q}}}\Bigr\}\Bigr)+C\kappa\mathfrak{L}_{q}^{2}\,.] 







@S6.SS1.p6 

@S6.SS1.p6.1 Although the statement of Theorem 6.1 is cumbersome, it is quite powerful: it states that once we have a smoothed sampler with Wasserstein guarantees, we can immediately obtain a stronger guarantee with a negligible overhead.
Indeed, the cost of the warm start generation is essentially the cost of the original smoothed sampler, called with a slightly smaller accuracy  [\Delta/\sqrt{q\kappa}]  up to logarithmic factors.
Crucially, in case (ii), we only need the smoothed sampler
guarantee in  [W_{p}]  for an order  [p]  which is logarithmic in
 [1/\delta] .


@S6.SS1.p7 

@S6.SS1.p7.1 Due to the use of the outer proximal sampler wrapper, note that the smoothed sampler is only ever called on distributions with condition number bounded by a universal constant; above we take it to be  [6] . This will ultimately improve the condition number dependence of our combined sampler.



@S6.SS2 

6.2 Technical tools


@S6.SS2.SSS1 

6.2.1 Regularization and reverse transport inequalities


@S6.SS2.SSS1.p1 

@S6.SS2.SSS1.p1.1 The key to the recursive warm start is the regularizing property of the heat flow, which enables proving reverse transport inequalities. We collect together the key inequalities here.


@S6.SS2.SSS1.p2 

@S6.SS2.SSS1.p2.1 We begin with a simple lemma on truncation.


@S6.Thmtheorem2 
Lemma 6.2 (Truncation).

@S6.Thmtheorem2.p1 

@S6.Thmtheorem2.p1.1 Let  [P,Q]  be probability laws satisfying
 [W_{p}(P,Q)\leqslant\varepsilon_{p}]  for some  [p\geqslant 2] . For every  [0<\delta<1] ,
there is a probability law  [P^{\dagger}]  such that





 [\mathsf{TV}(P,P^{\dagger})\leqslant\delta\,,\qquad W_{\infty}(P^{\dagger},Q)\leqslant\varepsilon_{p}\delta^{-1/p}\,.] 





@S6.SS2.SSS1.2 
Proof.

@S6.SS2.SSS1.p3 

@S6.SS2.SSS1.p3.1 Choose an optimal  [W_{p}]  coupling  [(Y,Y^{\star})]  of  [P]  and  [Q] . Define





 [Y^{\dagger}\coloneqq\begin{cases}Y\,,&\lVert Y-Y^{\star}\rVert\leqslant\varepsilon_{p}\delta^{-1/p}\,,\\
Y^{\star}\,,&\lVert Y-Y^{\star}\rVert>\varepsilon_{p}\delta^{-1/p}\,.\end{cases}] 



@S6.SS2.SSS1.p3.2 Markov’s inequality shows that  [Y\neq Y^{\dagger}]  with probability at most
 [\delta] , whereas
 [\lVert Y^{\dagger}-Y^{\star}\rVert\leqslant\varepsilon_{p}\delta^{-1/p}]  almost surely. Taking
 [P^{\dagger}\coloneqq\law(Y^{\dagger})]  proves the result.
∎



@S6.SS2.SSS1.p4 

@S6.SS2.SSS1.p4.1 For  [\tau>0] , let  [\gamma_{\tau}\coloneqq\mathsf{N}(0,\tau I)] .
The following lemma shows that after applying the heat flow, we can bound stronger information-theoretic divergences in terms of transport distances.


@S6.Thmtheorem3 
Lemma 6.3 (Reverse transport along the heat flow).

@S6.Thmtheorem3.p1 

@S6.Thmtheorem3.p1.1 Let  [P,Q]  be probability laws on  [\mathbb{R}^{d}] .

(i) 

@S6.I3.i1.p1 

@S6.I3.i1.p1.1 If  [P,Q\in\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})] , then





 [\KL(P*\gamma_{\tau}\mathbin{\|}Q*\gamma_{\tau})\leqslant\frac{W_{2}^{2}(P,Q)}{2\tau}\,.] 



(ii) 

@S6.I3.i2.p1 

@S6.I3.i2.p1.1 If  [P,Q\in\mathcal{P}_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})] , then for every
 [0<\delta<1] , there is a probability law  [P^{\dagger}]  such that





 [\mathsf{TV}(P,P^{\dagger})\leqslant\delta\,,\qquad\mathsf{R}_{q}(P^{\dagger}*\gamma_{\tau}\mathbin{\|}Q*\gamma_{\tau})\leqslant\frac{qW_{p}^{2}(P,Q)}{2\delta^{2/p}\tau}\,,\qquad\text{for all}~q>1\,.] 



(iii) 

@S6.I3.i3.p1 

@S6.I3.i3.p1.1 Let  [q\geqslant 2]  and suppose that
 [W_{\psi_{2}}(P,Q)\leqslant\varepsilon_{\psi_{2}}] . Then,





 [\frac{q\,(q-1)\,\varepsilon_{\psi_{2}}^{2}}{2\tau}\leqslant 1\implies\mathsf{R}_{q}(P*\gamma_{\tau}\mathbin{\|}Q*\gamma_{\tau})\leqslant\frac{q\log 2}{2\tau}\,\varepsilon_{\psi_{2}}^{2}\,.] 







@S6.SS2.SSS1.3 
Proof.

@S6.SS2.SSS1.p5 

@S6.SS2.SSS1.p5.1 Statement (i) follows from the joint convexity of the KL divergence.


@S6.SS2.SSS1.p6 

@S6.SS2.SSS1.p6.1 For (ii), choose  [P^{\dagger}]  as in Lemma 6.2 and use the fact that





 [\mathsf{R}_{q}(P^{\dagger}*\gamma_{\tau}\mathbin{\|}Q*\gamma_{\tau})\leqslant\frac{q}{2\tau}\,W_{\infty}^{2}(P^{\dagger},Q)\,.] 




@S6.SS2.SSS1.p7 

@S6.SS2.SSS1.p7.1 Part (iii) is [2, Remark 3.8], with the regularization
parameter there specialized to zero, with zero initial shift,
the source displacement parameter equal to  [W_{\psi_{2}}(P,Q)] , and
 [\sigma^{2}=t] .
∎




@S6.SS2.SSS2 

6.2.2 RGO calculus


@S6.SS2.SSS2.p1 

@S6.SS2.SSS2.p1.1 For  [U\in C^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})] ,  [A\in(0,\infty]] , and  [u\in\mathbb{R}^{d}] , define the RGO potential





 [U_{A,u}\coloneqq U+\frac{\lVert\cdot-u\rVert^{2}}{2A}\,,] 



@S6.SS2.SSS2.p1.2 and let  [R^{U}_{A,u}\propto\exp(-U_{A,u})]  be the corresponding RGO.


@S6.SS2.SSS2.p2 

@S6.SS2.SSS2.p2.1 In the lemma below, we note that the RGO of an RGO is itself an RGO for the original distribution, and we record how the parameters change. The proof is an elementary calculation and is omitted.


@S6.Thmtheorem4 
Lemma 6.4 (RGO calculus).

@S6.Thmtheorem4.p1 

@S6.Thmtheorem4.p1.2 Suppose that  [U]  satisfies
 [\kappa^{-1}I\preceq\nabla^{2}U\preceq I] .
Then,  [U_{A,u}]  is  [\alpha_{A}] -strongly convex and  [\beta_{A}] -smooth, where





 [\alpha_{A}\coloneqq\kappa^{-1}+A^{-1}\,,\qquad\beta_{A}\coloneqq 1+A^{-1}\,,\qquad\kappa_{A}\coloneqq\frac{\beta_{A}}{\alpha_{A}}\,.] 



@S6.Thmtheorem4.p1.3 Moreover,





 [R^{U_{A,u}}_{a,y}=R^{U}_{A^{+},u^{+}}\,,] 



@S6.Thmtheorem4.p1.4 where



@S6.E1X 

 [\displaystyle\frac{1}{A^{+}}] 
 [\displaystyle\coloneqq\frac{1}{A}+\frac{1}{a}\,,\qquad u^{+}\coloneqq A^{+}\,\Bigl(\frac{u}{A}+\frac{y}{a}\Bigr)\,,\qquad\kappa_{A^{+}}=\frac{a\beta_{A}+1}{a\beta_{A}+\kappa_{A}}\,\kappa_{A}\,.] 

(6.1)






@S6.SS3 

6.3 Recursive RGO sampler


@S6.SS3.p1 

@S6.SS3.p1.1 The following result gives the formal guarantee for
Algorithm 3.3.
This is essentially the same as Theorem 6.1, but without the outer proximal sampler wrapper.


@S6.Thmtheorem5 
Theorem 6.5 (Recursive RGO sampler).

@S6.Thmtheorem5.p1 

@S6.Thmtheorem5.p1.1 Assume (1.1), and suppose that the sampler  [\mathfrak{A}] 
satisfies one of

                    
                      A1
                    
                  , 
                    
                      A2
                    
                  , or

                    
                      A3
                    
                  . Fix  [A_{0}\in(0,\infty]] ,  [u_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}] , and correspondingly a target  [R^{V}_{A_{0},u_{0}}]  for which an admissible reference point  [x_{\rm ref}]  is
supplied, let  [\kappa_{0}\coloneqq\kappa_{A_{0}}] , and fix
 [0<\delta_{0},\Delta_{0}\leqslant 1/2] . For  [q\geqslant 2] , write





 [\mathfrak{L}_{q}\coloneqq q+\log\frac{\kappa_{0}dq}{\Delta_{0}}\,.] 



@S6.Thmtheorem5.p1.2 Then
Algorithm 3.3 has the following guarantees.

(i) 

@S6.I4.i1.p1 

@S6.I4.i1.p1.1 Under 
                    
                      A1
                    
                  , it returns a sample with law
 [\widehat{R}^{V}_{A_{0},u_{0}}]  satisfying





 [\KL(\widehat{R}^{V}_{A_{0},u_{0}}\mathbin{\|}R^{V}_{A_{0},u_{0}})\leqslant\Delta_{0}^{2}\,.] 



@S6.I4.i1.p1.2 The expected total number of gradient queries is at most





 [C\mathfrak{L}_{2}\,\mathsf{C}_{2}\Bigl(2\kappa_{0},d,\frac{c\Delta_{0}}{\sqrt{\mathfrak{L}_{2}}}\Bigr)+C\kappa_{0}\mathfrak{L}_{2}\,.] 



(ii) 

@S6.I4.i2.p1 

@S6.I4.i2.p1.1 Fix  [q\geqslant 2] .
Under 
                    
                      A2
                    
                  , it returns a sample with law
 [\widehat{R}^{V}_{A_{0},u_{0}}]  for which there exists
 [(\widehat{R}^{V}_{A_{0},u_{0}})^{\dagger}]  satisfying





 [\mathsf{TV}\bigl(\widehat{R}^{V}_{A_{0},u_{0}},(\widehat{R}^{V}_{A_{0},u_{0}})^{\dagger}\bigr)\leqslant\delta_{0}\,,\qquad\mathsf{R}_{q}\bigl((\widehat{R}^{V}_{A_{0},u_{0}})^{\dagger}\bigm\|R^{V}_{A_{0},u_{0}}\bigr)\leqslant\Delta_{0}^{2}\,.] 



@S6.I4.i2.p1.2 The expected total number of gradient queries is at most





 [C\mathfrak{L}_{q}\,\mathsf{C}_{p}\Bigl(2\kappa_{0},d,\frac{c\Delta_{0}}{\sqrt{q\mathfrak{L}_{q}}}\Bigr)+C\kappa_{0}\mathfrak{L}_{q}\,,] 



@S6.I4.i2.p1.3 where  [p\coloneqq 2\vee\lceil\log(C\mathfrak{L}_{q}/\delta_{0})\rceil] .

(iii) 

@S6.I4.i3.p1 

@S6.I4.i3.p1.1 Fix  [q\geqslant 2] . Under 
                    
                      A3
                    
                  , it returns a sample
with law  [\widehat{R}^{V}_{A_{0},u_{0}}]  satisfying





 [\mathsf{R}_{q}(\widehat{R}^{V}_{A_{0},u_{0}}\mathbin{\|}R^{V}_{A_{0},u_{0}})\leqslant\Delta_{0}^{2}\,.] 



@S6.I4.i3.p1.2 The expected total number of gradient queries is at most





 [C\mathfrak{L}_{q}\,\mathsf{C}_{\psi_{2}}\Bigl(2\kappa_{0},d,c\min\Bigl\{\frac{1}{q},\frac{\Delta_{0}}{\sqrt{q\mathfrak{L}_{q}}}\Bigr\}\Bigr)+C\kappa_{0}\mathfrak{L}_{q}\,.] 







@S6.SS3.p2 

@S6.SS3.p2.1 Although Algorithm 3.3 is written recursively, it is convenient to analyze it iteratively.
Thus, we break down the algorithm into stages, so that the target at stage  [j]  is  [V_{A_{j},u_{j}}] .
Let  [\eta_{j}] 
be the reported smoothing level at stage  [j] , and denote the rescaled output by
 [X_{j}] ; its law approximates  [R^{V}_{A_{j},u_{j}}*\mathsf{N}(0,\beta_{A_{j}}^{-1}\eta_{j}I)] 
in a Wasserstein metric. Draw an independent
 [G_{j}\sim\mathsf{N}(0,I)]  and set





 [Y_{j}\coloneqq X_{j}+\sqrt{\frac{\tau_{j}}{\beta_{A_{j}}}}\,G_{j}\,,\qquad a_{j}\coloneqq\frac{\eta_{j}+\tau_{j}}{\beta_{A_{j}}}\,.] 



@S6.SS3.p2.2 Thus,  [\law(Y_{j})]  is close to  [R_{A_{j},u_{j}}^{V}*\mathsf{N}(0,a_{j}I)] .
The algorithm then updates  [(A_{j+1},u_{j+1})]  by
(6.1) with RGO variance  [a_{j}]  and center  [Y_{j}] , as
in Algorithm 3.3. At the terminal level  [J] ,
it samples the remaining target  [R^{V}_{A_{J},u_{J}}]  with the FORS subroutine.


@S6.SS3.p3 

@S6.SS3.p3.1 At stage  [j] , write  [\kappa_{j}\coloneqq\kappa_{A_{j}}]  and choose the heat flow
duration





 [\tau_{j}\coloneqq\begin{cases}\kappa_{j}\,,&\kappa_{j}\geqslant 2\,,\\
c_{0}\,,&\kappa_{j}<2\,.\end{cases}] 

(6.2)



@S6.Thmtheorem6 
Lemma 6.6 (Geometric progress of the recursive stages).

@S6.Thmtheorem6.p1 

@S6.Thmtheorem6.p1.1 Suppose that  [c_{0}\in(0,1/4)]  and
 [0<\eta_{j}\leqslant c_{0}]  at every stage.

(i) 

@S6.I5.i1.p1 

@S6.I5.i1.p1.1 If  [\kappa_{j}\geqslant 2] , then





 [\frac{1}{2}\,\kappa_{j}\leqslant\kappa_{j+1}\leqslant\frac{4}{5}\,\kappa_{j}\,.] 

(6.3)


(ii) 

@S6.I5.i2.p1 

@S6.I5.i2.p1.1 If  [\kappa_{j}<2] , then  [A_{j+1}\leqslant 2c_{0}] , and all later
well-conditioned stages obey





 [A_{j+1}\leqslant\rho_{0}A_{j}\,,\qquad\rho_{0}\coloneqq\frac{2c_{0}}{1+2c_{0}}<1\,.] 







@S6.SS3.2 
Proof.

@S6.SS3.p4 

@S6.SS3.p4.1 In the first case, by
(6.2),  [\kappa_{j}=\tau_{j}\leqslant\eta_{j}+\tau_{j}\leqslant\kappa_{j}+c_{0}] .
Substitution in
(6.1) gives
(6.3) after decreasing  [c_{0}]  if necessary.


@S6.SS3.p5 

@S6.SS3.p5.1 In the well-conditioned regime,
 [c_{0}\leqslant\eta_{j}+\tau_{j}\leqslant 2c_{0}] .
By (6.1), the definition of  [a_{j}] , and
 [A_{j}\beta_{A_{j}}=A_{j}+1] ,





 [A_{j+1}=\frac{A_{j}a_{j}}{A_{j}+a_{j}}=\frac{A_{j}\,(\eta_{j}+\tau_{j})}{A_{j}\beta_{A_{j}}+\eta_{j}+\tau_{j}}=\frac{A_{j}\,(\eta_{j}+\tau_{j})}{A_{j}+1+\eta_{j}+\tau_{j}}\,.] 



@S6.SS3.p5.2 This yields





 [\displaystyle A_{j+1}] 
 [\displaystyle\leqslant\tau_{j}+\eta_{j}\leqslant 2c_{0}\,,\qquad\frac{A_{j+1}}{A_{j}}=\frac{\eta_{j}+\tau_{j}}{A_{j}+\eta_{j}+\tau_{j}+1}\leqslant\frac{2c_{0}}{1+2c_{0}}\,.\qed] 





@S6.SS3.3 
Proof of Theorem 6.5.

@S6.SS3.p6 

@S6.SS3.p6.1 Recursive error propagation.
Here and below, take  [q=2]  under 
                    
                      A1
                    
                  . For sufficiently small and
large universal constants  [c,C>0] , respectively, set





 [\underline{A}\coloneqq\frac{c}{\sqrt{d\mathfrak{L}_{q}}+\mathfrak{L}_{q}}\,,\qquad J\coloneqq\Bigl\lceil C\log\frac{e\kappa_{0}}{\underline{A}}\Bigr\rceil\leqslant C\mathfrak{L}_{q}\,.] 

(6.4)


@S6.SS3.p6.2 At every stage
 [j\in\{0,\ldots,J-1\}] , call the smoothed sampler with accuracy





 [\displaystyle\varepsilon_{2,j}^{2}] 
 [\displaystyle\coloneqq\frac{2\tau_{j}\Delta_{0}^{2}}{\kappa_{j}\,(J+1)}] 

 [\displaystyle\text{under \ref{ass:smoothed-w2}}\,,] 

(6.5)



 [\displaystyle\varepsilon_{p,j}^{2}] 
 [\displaystyle\coloneqq\frac{2e^{-2}\tau_{j}\Delta_{0}^{2}}{q\kappa_{j}\,(J+1)}\,,\qquad p\coloneqq 2\vee\Bigl\lceil\log\frac{C\mathfrak{L}_{q}}{\delta_{0}}\Bigr\rceil] 

 [\displaystyle\text{under \ref{ass:smoothed-wp}}\,,] 

(6.6)



 [\displaystyle\varepsilon_{\psi_{2},j}^{2}] 
 [\displaystyle\coloneqq\frac{c\tau_{j}}{\kappa_{j}}\min\Bigl\{\frac{1}{q\,(q-1)},\frac{\Delta_{0}^{2}}{q\,(J+1)}\Bigr\}] 

 [\displaystyle\text{under \ref{ass:smoothed-psi2}}\,.] 

(6.7)


@S6.SS3.p6.3 Fix a stage  [j<J]  and condition on the complete history up to that stage.
Write  [\widehat{P}_{j}]  for the conditional law of  [Y_{j}] , and
write  [P_{j}\coloneqq R^{V}_{A_{j},u_{j}}*\mathsf{N}(0,a_{j}I)] 
for its ideal law.
Also, let  [K_{j}(y,\cdot)\coloneqq R^{V_{A_{j},u_{j}}}_{a_{j},y}] ,
and let  [\widehat{K}_{j}(y,\cdot)]  be the conditional law returned by the
remaining recursive call after the center  [y]  is selected.
Thus,  [R_{j}\coloneqq R^{V}_{A_{j},u_{j}}=P_{j}K_{j}] , and the
output law from level  [j]  is  [\widehat{R}_{j}=\widehat{P}_{j}\widehat{K}_{j}] .
We prove by backward induction that, conditional on every history,





 [\displaystyle\KL(\widehat{R}_{j}\mathbin{\|}R_{j})] 
 [\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{J-j+1}{J+1}\,\Delta_{0}^{2}] 

 [\displaystyle\text{under \ref{ass:smoothed-w2}}\,,] 




 [\displaystyle\mathsf{R}_{q}(\widehat{R}_{j}^{\dagger}\mathbin{\|}R_{j})] 
 [\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{J-j+1}{J+1}\,\Delta_{0}^{2}\,,\qquad\mathsf{TV}(\widehat{R}_{j},\widehat{R}_{j}^{\dagger})\leqslant\frac{J-j}{J}\,\delta_{0}] 

 [\displaystyle\text{under \ref{ass:smoothed-wp}}\,,] 




 [\displaystyle\mathsf{R}_{q}(\widehat{R}_{j}\mathbin{\|}R_{j})] 
 [\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{J-j+1}{J+1}\,\Delta_{0}^{2}] 

 [\displaystyle\text{under \ref{ass:smoothed-psi2}}\,.] 



@S6.SS3.p6.4 The terminal construction below establishes the base case  [j=J] , with
 [\widehat{R}_{J}^{\dagger}=\widehat{R}_{J}]  under

                    
                      A2
                    
                  .


@S6.SS3.p7 

@S6.SS3.p7.1 Suppose that these inequalities are valid at stage  [j+1] . Under

                    
                      A1
                    
                  , after rescaling the output, the smoothed sampler provides the guarantee  [W_{2}^{2}\leqslant\kappa_{j}\varepsilon_{2,j}^{2}/\beta_{A_{j}}] .
After convolving with
 [\mathsf{N}(0,\beta_{A_{j}}^{-1}\tau_{j}I)] , part (i) of
Lemma 6.3 and
(6.5) yield





 [\KL(\widehat{P}_{j}\mathbin{\|}P_{j})\leqslant\frac{\kappa_{j}\varepsilon_{2,j}^{2}/\beta_{A_{j}}}{2\tau_{j}/\beta_{A_{j}}}=\frac{\kappa_{j}\varepsilon_{2,j}^{2}}{2\tau_{j}}=\frac{\Delta_{0}^{2}}{J+1}\,.] 



@S6.SS3.p7.2 Under 
                    
                      A2
                    
                  , the corresponding guarantee is
 [W_{p}^{2}\leqslant\kappa_{j}\varepsilon_{p,j}^{2}/\beta_{A_{j}}] . Apply
part (ii) of the same lemma with TV budget  [\delta_{0}/J] . Since
 [p\geqslant\log(J/\delta_{0})] , we have
 [(J/\delta_{0})^{2/p}\leqslant e^{2}] . Hence there exists  [\widehat{P}_{j}^{\dagger}]  with





 [\mathsf{TV}(\widehat{P}_{j},\widehat{P}_{j}^{\dagger})\leqslant\frac{\delta_{0}}{J}\,,\qquad\mathsf{R}_{q}(\widehat{P}_{j}^{\dagger}\mathbin{\|}P_{j})\leqslant\frac{qe^{2}\kappa_{j}\varepsilon_{p,j}^{2}/\beta_{A_{j}}}{2\tau_{j}/\beta_{A_{j}}}=\frac{qe^{2}\kappa_{j}\varepsilon_{p,j}^{2}}{2\tau_{j}}=\frac{\Delta_{0}^{2}}{J+1}\,.] 



@S6.SS3.p7.3 Under 
                    
                      A3
                    
                  , the corresponding guarantee is
 [W_{\psi_{2}}^{2}\leqslant\kappa_{j}\varepsilon_{\psi_{2},j}^{2}/\beta_{A_{j}}] .
For a sufficiently small universal  [c] ,
(6.7) verifies both





 [\frac{q(q-1)\kappa_{j}\varepsilon_{\psi_{2},j}^{2}/\beta_{A_{j}}}{2\tau_{j}/\beta_{A_{j}}}=\frac{q(q-1)\kappa_{j}\varepsilon_{\psi_{2},j}^{2}}{2\tau_{j}}\leqslant 1\qquad\text{and}\qquad\mathsf{R}_{q}(\widehat{P}_{j}\mathbin{\|}P_{j})\leqslant\frac{\Delta_{0}^{2}}{J+1}\,.] 



@S6.SS3.p7.4 We now verify the induction step. Under 
                    
                      A1
                    
                  , the KL
chain rule and data processing give





 [\displaystyle\KL(\widehat{R}_{j}\mathbin{\|}R_{j})] 
 [\displaystyle\leqslant\KL(\widehat{P}_{j}\mathbin{\|}P_{j})+\int\KL\bigl(\widehat{K}_{j}(y,\cdot)\bigm\|K_{j}(y,\cdot)\bigr)\,\widehat{P}_{j}(\mathrm{d}y)] 





 [\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{\Delta_{0}^{2}}{J+1}+\frac{J-j}{J+1}\,\Delta_{0}^{2}=\frac{J-j+1}{J+1}\,\Delta_{0}^{2}\,.] 



@S6.SS3.p7.5 Under 
                    
                      A3
                    
                  , the same argument applies except that we replace the KL chain rule with the Rényi composition rule.
Finally, under 
                    
                      A2
                    
                  , we take  [\widehat{R}_{j}^{\dagger}\coloneqq\widehat{P}_{j}^{\dagger}\widehat{K}_{j}^{\dagger}] , and we obtain the bound on  [\mathsf{R}_{q}(\widehat{R}_{j}^{\dagger}\mathbin{\|}R_{j})]  as before.
Also, we maximally couple  [\widehat{P}_{j}]  and
 [\widehat{P}_{j}^{\dagger}] , and on the event that their draws agree, we use the inductive
coupling of  [\widehat{K}_{j}^{\dagger}]  and  [\widehat{K}_{j}] . This gives





 [\displaystyle\mathsf{TV}(\widehat{R}_{j},\widehat{R}_{j}^{\dagger})] 
 [\displaystyle\leqslant\mathsf{TV}(\widehat{P}_{j},\widehat{P}_{j}^{\dagger})+\sup_{y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\mathsf{TV}\bigl(\widehat{K}_{j}(y,\cdot),\widehat{K}_{j}^{\dagger}(y,\cdot)\bigr)\leqslant\frac{\delta_{0}}{J}+\frac{J-j-1}{J}\,\delta_{0}=\frac{J-j}{J}\,\delta_{0}\,.] 



@S6.SS3.p7.6 This completes the induction.


@S6.SS3.p8 

@S6.SS3.p8.1 Reference point construction.
We verify that every smoothed sampler call is supplied with an admissible
reference point and account for the work needed to construct these points.
Let  [U]  be the current normalized potential and  [Y]  the output of the
smoothed sampler, and write  [\tau]  for the current heat flow duration.
Under 
                    
                      A1
                    
                   and 
                    
                      A2
                    
                  , use  [W_{2}\leqslant W_{p}] .
Under 
                    
                      A3
                    
                  , Jensen’s inequality in the definition of
 [W_{\psi_{2}}]  gives  [W_{2}\leqslant\sqrt{\log 2}\,W_{\psi_{2}}] . Thus, in every
case, we can couple  [Y]  to the ideal
 [Y^{\star}=X+\sqrt{\eta}\,G^{\prime}] , where  [X\sim\pi_{U}] .
This yields, by  [1] -smoothness of  [U] ,





 [\mathbb{E}\lVert\nabla U(Y)\rVert^{2}\leqslant 2\,\bigl(\mathbb{E}\lVert\nabla U(Y^{\star})\rVert^{2}+\kappa_{U}\varepsilon_{j}^{2}\bigr)\leqslant C\,(d+\eta d+\kappa_{U}\varepsilon_{j}^{2})\,,] 



@S6.SS3.p8.2 where  [\varepsilon_{j}]  denotes the accuracy at stage  [j] .


@S6.SS3.p9 

@S6.SS3.p9.1 In normalized coordinates, the next RGO potential is





 [U^{+}\coloneqq U+\frac{\lVert\cdot-(Y+\sqrt{\tau}\,G)\rVert^{2}}{2\,(\eta+\tau)}\,.] 



@S6.SS3.p9.2 Since
 [\kappa_{U}^{-1}I\preceq\nabla^{2}U\preceq I] , the potential  [U^{+}]  is
 [\alpha^{+}] -strongly convex and  [\beta^{+}] -smooth, where





 [\alpha^{+}\coloneqq\kappa_{U}^{-1}+(\eta+\tau)^{-1}\,,\qquad\beta^{+}\coloneqq 1+(\eta+\tau)^{-1}\,.] 



@S6.SS3.p9.3 Its gradient at  [Y]  adds only
 [-\sqrt{\tau}\,G/(\eta+\tau)] , and hence





 [\mathbb{E}\lVert\nabla U^{+}(Y)\rVert^{2}\leqslant C\,\Bigl(d+\kappa_{U}\varepsilon_{j}^{2}+\frac{\tau d}{(\eta+\tau)^{2}}\Bigr)\,.] 



@S6.SS3.p9.4 Gradient descent with step size  [1/\beta^{+}]  produces iterates  [\{x_{k}\}_{k\geqslant 0}]  with





 [\lVert\nabla U^{+}(x_{k})\rVert^{2}\leqslant\kappa^{+}\,\Bigl(1-\frac{1}{\kappa^{+}}\Bigr)^{k}\,\lVert\nabla U^{+}(Y)\rVert^{2}\,,\qquad\kappa^{+}\coloneqq\frac{\beta^{+}}{\alpha^{+}}\,.] 



@S6.SS3.p9.5 Stopping when  [\lVert\nabla U^{+}(x_{k})\rVert\leqslant\sqrt{\alpha^{+}d}]  gives the admissible reference point for the next stage. The requested radii make the
initial squared gradient polynomial in the stage parameters, so Jensen’s
inequality bounds the expected work at this stage by
 [\widetilde{O}(\kappa^{+})] . The condition numbers in the
large conditioning regime form a decreasing geometric sequence, and all later
stages have universally bounded condition number. Summing over the stages
in (6.4) bounds the expected cost of all inner reference
points by  [C\kappa_{0}\mathfrak{L}_{q}] .


@S6.SS3.p10 

@S6.SS3.p10.1 Terminal stage.
By Lemma 6.6(i), the regime
 [\kappa_{j}\geqslant 2]  lasts for at most  [C\log(e\kappa_{0})]  stages. Thereafter,
part (ii) of the same lemma contracts  [A_{j}]  by the fixed factor
 [\rho_{0}<1] . Thus (6.4) ensures that
 [A_{J}\leqslant\underline{A}] . Set
 [\varepsilon_{J}\coloneqq\Delta_{0}/\sqrt{J+1}] ; this is the accuracy requested from the
terminal FORS sampler.


@S6.SS3.p11 

@S6.SS3.p11.1 Starting from the retained reference point, gradient descent on the terminal
potential produces  [x_{+}]  satisfying  [\lVert\nabla V_{A_{J},u_{J}}(x_{+})\rVert\leqslant\sqrt{d/A_{J}}] .
The same descent estimate used for the inner reference points shows that
this terminal construction costs at most  [C\kappa_{0}\mathfrak{L}_{q}] 
expected gradient queries. Moreover,





 [\lVert u_{J}-A_{J}\nabla V(x_{+})-x_{+}\rVert=A_{J}\,\lVert\nabla V_{A_{J},u_{J}}(x_{+})\rVert\leqslant\sqrt{dA_{J}}\,,] 



@S6.SS3.p11.2 which is exactly the condition in Theorem A.4 with
target  [R^{V}_{A_{J},u_{J}}] .


@S6.SS3.p12 

@S6.SS3.p12.1 Under 
                    
                      A1
                    
                  , let  [\widehat{R}_{J}]  be the output of FORS at
order  [2] . Under 
                    
                      A2
                    
                  , let
 [\widehat{R}_{J}]  be the output of FORS at order  [q]  and set
 [\widehat{R}_{J}^{\dagger}=\widehat{R}_{J}] . Under

                    
                      A3
                    
                  , let  [\widehat{R}_{J}]  be the output of FORS at order
 [q] . The choice of  [\varepsilon_{J}]  verifies the localized-scale condition in Theorem A.4. Hence every terminal call
contributes at most  [\Delta_{0}^{2}/(J+1)]  in the relevant divergence and has
constant expected gradient query cost, establishing the base cases of the
backward induction.
∎




@S6.SS4 

6.4 Outer proximal sampler


@S6.SS4.p1 

@S6.SS4.p1.1 From Theorems 1.1 and 5.1, the smoothed Picard HMC sampler requires at least  [\kappa^{2}]  queries, and if we use it as our smoothed sampler then this cost is inherited by the recursive RGO sampler.
Instead, the full warm start generator runs an outer proximal chain with step size
 [(2\beta)^{-1}]  and applies the recursive RGO sampler only to its uniformly
well-conditioned RGO targets. This is the construction described in
Algorithm 3.4.


@S6.SS4.p2 

@S6.SS4.p2.1 Initialize
 [X_{0}\sim\widehat{\mu}_{0}\coloneqq\mathsf{N}(x_{\rm ref},(2\beta)^{-1}I)] .
An outer step draws
 [Y_{n}=X_{n}+\mathsf{N}(0,(2\beta)^{-1}I)] , and then applies an approximate RGO
kernel at  [Y_{n}] .


@S6.SS4.2 
Proof of Theorem 6.1.

@S6.SS4.p3 

@S6.SS4.p3.1 Under 
                    
                      A1
                    
                  , choose





 [\Delta_{0}^{2}\coloneqq\frac{c\Delta^{2}}{\kappa}\,,\qquad N\coloneqq\Bigl\lceil C\kappa\log\frac{ed\kappa}{\Delta}\Bigr\rceil\,.] 



@S6.SS4.p3.2 Under 
                    
                      A2
                    
                   and 
                    
                      A3
                    
                  , keep the
prescribed order  [q\geqslant 2] , and choose





 [\Delta_{0}^{2}\coloneqq\frac{c\Delta^{2}}{\kappa\log(eq)}\,,\qquad N_{0}\coloneqq\Bigl\lceil C\kappa\log\frac{ed\kappa}{\Delta}\Bigr\rceil\,,\qquad N_{1}\coloneqq\Bigl\lceil\frac{\log(q-1)}{\log(1+(2\kappa)^{-1})}\Bigr\rceil\,,\qquad N\coloneqq N_{0}+N_{1}\,.] 




@S6.SS4.p4 

@S6.SS4.p4.1 Initialization.
Write  [V_{\star}\coloneqq\inf V]  and  [g=\nabla V(x_{\rm ref})] . The
logarithmic Sobolev inequality implies the Poincaré inequality, and hence
 [\operatorname{Cov}_{\pi}\preceq\alpha^{-1}I] . The Gaussian
maximum entropy bound, together with
 [-\int\log\pi\,\mathrm{d}\pi\geqslant V_{\star}+\log\int e^{-V}] ,
therefore gives





 [\log\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}e^{-V}+V_{\star}\leqslant\frac{d}{2}\log\frac{2\uppi e}{\alpha}\,.] 



@S6.SS4.p4.2 On the other hand,  [\beta] -smoothness yields





 [V(x)\leqslant V(x_{\rm ref})+\langle g,x-x_{\rm ref}\rangle+\frac{\beta}{2}\,\lVert x-x_{\rm ref}\rVert^{2}] 



@S6.SS4.p4.3 and by the descent lemma,





 [\frac{\lVert g\rVert^{2}}{2\beta}\leqslant V(x_{\rm ref})-V_{\star}\leqslant d\,.] 



@S6.SS4.p4.4 Comparing the density of
 [\widehat{\mu}_{0}=\mathsf{N}(x_{\rm ref},(2\beta)^{-1}I)]  with that of
 [\pi] , and maximizing the resulting quadratic upper bound, gives the
pointwise estimate





 [\log\frac{\mathrm{d}\widehat{\mu}_{0}}{\mathrm{d}\pi}\leqslant\frac{d}{2}\log(2e\kappa)+2d\leqslant Cd\log(e\kappa)\,.] 



@S6.SS4.p4.5 Consequently,
 [\mathsf{R}_{q}(\widehat{\mu}_{0}\mathbin{\|}\pi)\leqslant Cd\log(e\kappa)]  for every
 [q\geqslant 1] , with the convention  [\mathsf{R}_{1}=\KL] .


@S6.SS4.p5 

@S6.SS4.p5.1 Approximate RGO kernels.
For every outer RGO target, apply
Theorem 6.5 with the local accuracy  [\Delta_{0}] 
chosen above. Under

                    
                      A2
                    
                  , use  [\delta_{0}=\delta/(2N)] . Every outer RGO
has curvature between  [\beta I]  and  [3\beta I] . Apply
Theorem 6.5 to sample from the RGO, taking  [A_{0}=\infty] . Thus, its three explicit bounds
apply with  [\kappa_{0}\leqslant 3] , uniformly over the outer center and complete
history.


@S6.SS4.p6 

@S6.SS4.p6.1 Outer contraction and error propagation.
Let  [\widehat{\mu}_{n}]  denote the law of the  [n] -th iterate  [X_{n}]  of the proximal sampler,
and, under 
                    
                      A2
                    
                  , let  [\mu_{n}^{\dagger}] 
denote the comparison law. Write
 [\mu_{n}^{\sharp}=\widehat{\mu}_{n}]  under 
                    
                      A1
                    
                   and

                    
                      A3
                    
                  , and  [\mu_{n}^{\sharp}=\mu_{n}^{\dagger}]  under

                    
                      A2
                    
                  .


@S6.SS4.p7 

@S6.SS4.p7.1 Under 
                    
                      A1
                    
                  , the forward half of the proximal sampler
contraction in [19, Theorem 3 and Appendix A.4] and the KL chain rule give





 [\KL(\widehat{\mu}_{n+1}\mathbin{\|}\pi)\leqslant(1+(2\kappa)^{-1})^{-1}\KL(\widehat{\mu}_{n}\mathbin{\|}\pi)+\Delta_{0}^{2}\,.] 



@S6.SS4.p7.2 Consequently,





 [\KL(\widehat{\mu}_{N}\mathbin{\|}\pi)\leqslant(1+(2\kappa)^{-1})^{-N}\,Cd\log(e\kappa)+C\kappa\Delta_{0}^{2}\leqslant\Delta^{2}\,.] 




@S6.SS4.p8 

@S6.SS4.p8.1 It remains to treat 
                    
                      A2
                    
                   and

                    
                      A3
                    
                  . During the first  [N_{0}]  steps, use the
Rényi contraction from [19, Theorem 3 and
Appendix A.4] and Rényi composition. This yields





 [\mathsf{R}_{2}(\mu_{N_{0}}^{\sharp}\mathbin{\|}\pi)\leqslant(1+(2\kappa)^{-1})^{-N_{0}/2}\,Cd\log(e\kappa)+C\kappa\Delta_{0}^{2}\leqslant\frac{\Delta^{2}}{2}\,.] 



@S6.SS4.p8.2 For the remaining  [N_{1}]  steps, we leverage hypercontractivity to obtain a sharper dependence on  [q] . Define  [q_{N_{0}}\coloneqq 2]  and





 [q_{n+1}-1\coloneqq\min\{q-1,(1+(2\kappa)^{-1})\,(q_{n}-1)\}\,,\qquad n=N_{0},\ldots,N-1\,.] 



@S6.SS4.p8.3 Thus,  [q_{N}=q] . Since  [\pi]  has log-Sobolev constant at most
 [\alpha^{-1}] , hypercontractivity of simultaneous heat flow
[34, Theorem 1.2 and Corollary 1.3] gives





 [\mathsf{R}_{q_{n+1}}(\mu_{n}^{\sharp}*\gamma_{(2\beta)^{-1}}\mathbin{\|}\pi*\gamma_{(2\beta)^{-1}})\leqslant\mathsf{R}_{q_{n}}(\mu_{n}^{\sharp}\mathbin{\|}\pi)\,.] 



@S6.SS4.p8.4 Data processing through the exact backward RGO, followed by
Rényi composition with the approximate RGO kernel, therefore yields





 [\mathsf{R}_{q_{n+1}}(\mu_{n+1}^{\sharp}\mathbin{\|}\pi)\leqslant\mathsf{R}_{q_{n}}(\mu_{n}^{\sharp}\mathbin{\|}\pi)+\Delta_{0}^{2}\,.] 



@S6.SS4.p8.5 Iterating and using  [N_{1}\leqslant C\kappa\log(eq)] , we obtain





 [\mathsf{R}_{q}(\mu_{N}^{\sharp}\mathbin{\|}\pi)\leqslant\mathsf{R}_{2}(\mu_{N_{0}}^{\sharp}\mathbin{\|}\pi)+N_{1}\Delta_{0}^{2}\leqslant\Delta^{2}\,.] 



@S6.SS4.p8.6 Under 
                    
                      A2
                    
                  , the TV errors also sum to at most  [\delta/2] .


@S6.SS4.p9 

@S6.SS4.p9.1 Outer reference points.
Under 
                    
                      A1
                    
                   and 
                    
                      A3
                    
                  , the preceding
bounds on the KL divergence, followed by
Talagrand’s inequality and smoothness, bounds the second moment of
 [\nabla V(Y_{n})] . If we run gradient descent starting from  [Y_{n}] ,
Jensen’s inequality implies that we can find an admissible reference point to apply Theorem 6.5 using logarithmic expected work per
outer step.


@S6.SS4.p10 

@S6.SS4.p10.1 Under 
                    
                      A2
                    
                  , the implementable chain need not have
controlled moments of all orders, so cap the outer reference gradient descent after





 [N_{\rm cap}\coloneqq\Bigl\lceil\log_{2}\frac{C\kappa N}{\delta}\Bigr\rceil] 



@S6.SS4.p10.2 steps. Markov’s inequality shows that the
sum of the probabilities that we hit  [N_{\rm cap}]  iterations is at most  [\delta/2] . Together with TV bounds from the preceding argument, this gives
 [\mathsf{TV}(\widehat{\mu}_{N},\mu_{N}^{\dagger})\leqslant\delta] . Set
 [\widehat{\pi}\coloneqq\widehat{\mu}_{N}] , and, under 
                    
                      A2
                    
                  , set
 [\widehat{\pi}^{\dagger}\coloneqq\mu_{N}^{\dagger}] .


@S6.SS4.p11 

@S6.SS4.p11.1 Query cost.
The choice above satisfies  [N\leqslant C\kappa\mathfrak{L}_{2}]  under

                    
                      A1
                    
                  , and  [N\leqslant C\kappa\mathfrak{L}_{q}]  under

                    
                      A2
                    
                   and 
                    
                      A3
                    
                  .
Since the capped outer reference construction uses at most





 [C\kappa\mathfrak{L}_{q}\,\Bigl(\mathfrak{L}_{q}+\log\frac{C\kappa\mathfrak{L}_{q}}{\delta}\Bigr)] 



@S6.SS4.p11.2 queries, and in the other two cases the cost is at most the final displayed term, Theorem 6.5 and careful bookkeeping proves the result.
∎





@S7 

7 Picard HMC warm starts and the high-accuracy sampler


@S7.SS1 

7.1 Picard HMC warm starts


@S7.SS1.p1 

@S7.SS1.p1.1 We first state a strengthened form of Theorem 1.2. In
particular, strong log-concavity is replaced by log-smoothness and a logarithmic
Sobolev inequality, and we also state KL divergence guarantee.


@S7.Thmtheorem1 
Theorem 7.1 (Picard HMC warm starts).

@S7.Thmtheorem1.p1 

@S7.Thmtheorem1.p1.1 Suppose that  [\pi\propto\exp(-V)]  is  [\beta] -log-smooth and satisfies
the logarithmic Sobolev inequality with constant  [\alpha^{-1}] . Write
 [\kappa\coloneqq\beta/\alpha] , and suppose that we are given a point
 [x_{\rm ref}]  satisfying  [V(x_{\rm ref})-\inf V\leqslant d] .
For every  [0<\Delta,\delta\leqslant 1/2] , Picard HMC within the recursive warm start
generator satisfies the following guarantees.

(i) 

@S7.I1.i1.p1 

@S7.I1.i1.p1.1 It returns a sample with law  [\widehat{\pi}]  satisfying





 [\KL(\widehat{\pi}\mathbin{\|}\pi)\leqslant\Delta^{2}\,.] 



@S7.I1.i1.p1.2 The expected number of gradient queries is at most





 [C\,\bigl(\kappa^{7/6}d^{1/6}\Delta^{-1/3}+\kappa\bigr)\log^{9}\frac{e\kappa d}{\Delta}\,.] 



(ii) 

@S7.I1.i2.p1 

@S7.I1.i2.p1.1 It returns a sample with law  [\widehat{\pi}_{\delta}] 
for which there exists
 [\widehat{\pi}_{\delta}^{\dagger}]  satisfying





 [\mathsf{TV}(\widehat{\pi}_{\delta},\widehat{\pi}_{\delta}^{\dagger})\leqslant\delta\,,\qquad\mathsf{R}_{2}(\widehat{\pi}_{\delta}^{\dagger}\mathbin{\|}\pi)\leqslant\Delta^{2}\,.] 



@S7.I1.i2.p1.2 The expected number of gradient queries is at most





 [C\,\bigl(\kappa^{7/6}d^{1/6}\Delta^{-1/3}+\kappa\bigr)\log^{9}\frac{e\kappa d}{\Delta\delta}\,.] 







@S7.SS1.2 
Proof.

@S7.SS1.p2 

@S7.SS1.p2.1 We verify that Picard HMC implements 
                    
                      A1
                    
                   and

                    
                      A2
                    
                  , and then invoke
Theorem 6.1. Fix a normalized potential  [U]  with
condition number  [\kappa_{U}] , let  [p\geqslant 2] , and put  [\bar{\varepsilon}\coloneqq\min\{\varepsilon,\kappa_{U}^{-1/2}\}] .
Apply Theorem 5.1 with accuracy
 [\sqrt{\kappa_{U}}\,\bar{\varepsilon}] . It returns  [0<\eta\leqslant c_{0}]  and a law
 [\widehat{\pi}_{U,\eta}]  satisfying





 [\kappa_{U}^{-1/2}\,W_{p}(\widehat{\pi}_{U,\eta},\pi_{U,\eta})\leqslant\bar{\varepsilon}\leqslant\varepsilon\,.] 



@S7.SS1.p2.2 Thus Picard HMC implements 
                    
                      A2
                    
                   with complexity





 [\mathsf{C}_{p}(\kappa_{U},d,\varepsilon)\leqslant C\,\bigl(\kappa_{U}^{2}+\kappa_{U}^{7/6}\,(d+p)^{1/6}\,\bar{\varepsilon}^{-1/3}\bigr)\,\Bigl(p+\log\frac{e\sqrt{\kappa_{U}}\,d}{\bar{\varepsilon}}\Bigr)^{6}\,.] 



@S7.SS1.p2.3 Taking  [p=2]  also implements 
                    
                      A1
                    
                  .
Careful bookkeeping shows that the costs are as displayed.
∎



@S7.SS1.p3 

@S7.SS1.p3.1 Under (1.1), the reference assumption of
Theorem 1.2 implies





 [V(x_{\rm ref})-\inf V\leqslant\frac{\lVert\nabla V(x_{\rm ref})\rVert^{2}}{2\alpha}\leqslant\frac{d}{2}\,.] 



@S7.SS1.p3.2 Thus Theorem 1.2 follows from
Theorem 7.1(ii) by taking  [\Delta=1/2] . Part (i)
also gives a genuine KL warm start under the more general assumptions of
Theorem 7.1.



@S7.SS2 

7.2 From the proxy warm start to high accuracy


@S7.SS2.p1 

@S7.SS2.p1.1 We now record the guarantee for proximal BPS.


@S7.Thmtheorem2 
Theorem 7.2 (Proximal BPS).

@S7.Thmtheorem2.p1 

@S7.Thmtheorem2.p1.1 Assume  [V\in C^{2}] . Let  [\Delta\geqslant 1]  and  [0<\tau<1/4] , and set





 [\mathfrak{L}_{\mathrm{BPS}}\coloneqq\Delta^{2}+\log\frac{Cd\kappa}{\tau}\,.] 



@S7.Thmtheorem2.p1.2 Then, the proximal BPS Markov kernel
 [K_{\tau,\Delta}]  satisfies the following guarantee:
for every  [\lambda_{0}]  with
 [\mathsf{R}_{2}(\lambda_{0}\mathbin{\|}\pi)\leqslant\Delta^{2}] , it holds that  [\mathsf{TV}(\lambda_{0}K_{\tau,\Delta},\pi)\leqslant\tau] .
Moreover, if  [N_{\nabla V}]  is the total number of gradient queries made by this
kernel, then





 [\mathbb{E}N_{\nabla V}\leqslant C\sqrt{\kappa}\,\bigl(d\mathfrak{L}_{\mathrm{BPS}}+\mathfrak{L}_{\mathrm{BPS}}^{2}\bigr)^{1/4}\,\mathfrak{L}_{\mathrm{BPS}}^{3/2}\log(\kappa\mathfrak{L}_{\mathrm{BPS}})\,.] 

(7.1)




@S7.SS2.2 
Proof.

@S7.SS2.p2 

@S7.SS2.p2.1 This is Algorithm 4.2, Theorem 4.3, and Corollary 4.4 of
[16].
∎



@S7.SS2.3 
Proof of Theorem 1.3.

@S7.SS2.p3 

@S7.SS2.p3.1 Apply Theorem 7.1(ii) with
 [\Delta=1/2]  and  [\delta=\varepsilon/2] . This produces an
implementable law  [\widehat{\pi}_{\varepsilon/2}]  and a comparison law
 [\widehat{\pi}_{\varepsilon/2}^{\dagger}]  such that





 [\mathsf{TV}(\widehat{\pi}_{\varepsilon/2},\widehat{\pi}_{\varepsilon/2}^{\dagger})\leqslant\frac{\varepsilon}{2}\,,\qquad\mathsf{R}_{2}(\widehat{\pi}_{\varepsilon/2}^{\dagger}\mathbin{\|}\pi)\leqslant\frac{1}{4}\,.] 



@S7.SS2.p3.2 Run the proximal BPS kernel
 [K_{\varepsilon/2,1}]  from the implementable law. By
data processing,





 [\mathsf{TV}\bigl(\widehat{\pi}_{\varepsilon/2}K_{\varepsilon/2,1},\widehat{\pi}_{\varepsilon/2}^{\dagger}K_{\varepsilon/2,1}\bigr)\leqslant\frac{\varepsilon}{2}\,.] 



@S7.SS2.p3.3 Since  [\widehat{\pi}_{\varepsilon/2}^{\dagger}]  satisfies the hypothesis of
Theorem 7.2 with  [\Delta=1] , its image under this kernel is
within  [\varepsilon/2]  of  [\pi]  in total variation. The triangle
inequality therefore gives the desired error  [\varepsilon] .


@S7.SS2.p4 

@S7.SS2.p4.1 Put  [\mathfrak{L}\coloneqq\log(e\kappa d/\varepsilon)] . The warm start
cost is at most
 [C\kappa^{7/6}d^{1/6}\mathfrak{L}^{9}] , since  [d,\kappa\geqslant 1] .
For the subsequent proximal BPS call,  [\Delta=1]  and
 [\tau=\varepsilon/2] , so  [\mathfrak{L}_{\mathrm{BPS}}\leqslant C\mathfrak{L}] .
Thus (7.1) gives the cost bound





 [C\sqrt{\kappa}\,(d^{1/4}\mathfrak{L}^{7/4}+\mathfrak{L}^{2})\log(\kappa\mathfrak{L})\leqslant C\sqrt{\kappa}\,(d^{1/4}\mathfrak{L}^{11/4}+\mathfrak{L}^{3})\,.] 



@S7.SS2.p4.2 Here we used  [\log(\kappa\mathfrak{L})\leqslant C\mathfrak{L}] .
The remaining term  [\sqrt{\kappa}\,\mathfrak{L}^{3}]  is absorbed by
 [\kappa^{7/6}d^{1/6}\mathfrak{L}^{9}] , proving the stated query bound.
∎







@A1 

Appendix A Terminal RGO implementation by FORS


@A1.p1 

@A1.p1.1 This appendix records the FORS subroutine that we use in the paper. After reviewing the generic FORS mechanism,
we describe a variant that is tailored to sampling from RGO distributions.
Since the main arguments here were already introduced in prior works, we will be more terse and only emphasize the differences from the analyses of
[15, 17].


@A1.SS1 

A.1 Review of first-order rejection sampling (FORS)


@A1.SS1.p1 

@A1.SS1.p1.1 For  [q\geqslant 1] , write





 [\overline{\mathsf{D}}_{q}(P,Q)\coloneqq\max\Bigl\{\int\Bigl(\frac{\mathrm{d}P}{\mathrm{d}Q}\Bigr)^{q}\,\mathrm{d}Q-1\,,\int\Bigl(\frac{\mathrm{d}Q}{\mathrm{d}P}\Bigr)^{q}\,\mathrm{d}P-1\Bigr\}\,.] 



@A1.SS1.p1.2 Either term is understood to be infinite when the corresponding
absolute continuity condition fails.


@A1.SS1.p2 

@A1.SS1.p2.1 Let  [\mathbf{Q}]  be a proposal law for a path  [\mathbf{Z}] , and suppose the
desired path law satisfies





 [\frac{\mathrm{d}\mathbf{P}_{\star}}{\mathrm{d}\mathbf{Q}}(\mathbf{Z})\propto\exp\{w_{\star}(\mathbf{Z})\}\,.] 



@A1.SS1.p2.2 Given an auxiliary variable  [\xi\sim\Xi] , FORS uses an estimator
 [W(\xi;\mathbf{Z})]  with
 [w(\mathbf{Z})\coloneqq\mathbb{E}_{\xi\sim\Xi}W(\xi;\mathbf{Z})] . At clipping level
 [B>0] , its implemented path law  [\widehat{\mathbf{P}}]  satisfies





 [\frac{\mathrm{d}\widehat{\mathbf{P}}}{\mathrm{d}\mathbf{Q}}(\mathbf{Z})\propto\exp\bigl\{\mathbb{E}_{\xi\sim\Xi}\operatorname{clip}_{[-B,B]}\bigl(W(\xi;\mathbf{Z})\bigr)\bigr\}\,.] 




@A1.Thmtheorem1 
Lemma A.1 (FORS likelihood comparison).

@A1.Thmtheorem1.p1 

@A1.Thmtheorem1.p1.1 Let  [P_{\star}]  and  [\widehat{P}]  be the laws of the terminal endpoint of
 [\mathbf{Z}]  under  [\mathbf{P}_{\star}]  and  [\widehat{\mathbf{P}}] ,
respectively. For every  [q\geqslant 2] ,





 [\displaystyle 2\log\{1+\overline{\mathsf{D}}_{q}(\widehat{P},P_{\star})\}\leqslant{}] 
 [\displaystyle\log\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{P}_{\star}}\exp\bigl\{4q\,|w_{\star}(\mathbf{Z})-w(\mathbf{Z})|\bigr\}+\log\mathbb{E}_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{Z}\sim\mathbf{P}_{\star}\\
\xi\sim\Xi\end{subarray}}\exp\bigl\{4q\,\bigl(|W(\xi;\mathbf{Z})|-B\bigr)_{+}\bigr\}\,.] 





@A1.SS1.2 
Proof.

@A1.SS1.p3 

@A1.SS1.p3.1 This is [17, Lemma 2.4].
∎



@A1.SS1.p4 

@A1.SS1.p4.1 To draw a sample from the law  [\widehat{\mathbf{P}}] , we use the following mechanism.


@A1.Thmtheorem2 
Lemma A.2 (Poisson exponentiation).

@A1.Thmtheorem2.p1 

@A1.Thmtheorem2.p1.1 Let  [X\sim Q] . Suppose that, conditionally on  [X=x] , one can simulate a
random variable  [W_{x}\in[-B,B]]  with
 [w(x)\coloneqq\mathbb{E}[W_{x}\mid X=x]] . Draw
 [N\sim\mathsf{Poisson}(2B)]  and, conditionally on  [X=x] ,
independent copies  [W_{x}^{(1)},\ldots,W_{x}^{(N)}] . Accept  [x]  with
probability





 [\prod_{k=1}^{N}\frac{B+W_{x}^{(k)}}{2B}\,.] 



@A1.Thmtheorem2.p1.2 Then the accepted proposal has law proportional to
 [\exp(w)\,Q] . The acceptance probability of one attempt is
at least  [\exp(-2B)] .



@A1.SS1.3 
Proof.

@A1.SS1.p5 

@A1.SS1.p5.1 See [17, Algorithm 1 and Theorem 2.3].
∎




@A1.SS2 

A.2 FORS for RGO distributions


@A1.SS2.p1 

@A1.SS2.p1.1 Given a convex potential  [V] , a variance  [a>0] , and a center  [y] , let





 [R_{a,y}\propto\exp\Bigl\{-V(\cdot)-\frac{\lVert\cdot-y\rVert^{2}}{2a}\Bigr\}\,.] 



@A1.SS2.p1.2 Here, we describe a variant of FORS diffusion simulation, specialized to distributions of this form.
Compared to [17], we exactly integrate the
linear part of the drift in the underdamped Langevin proposal.
Namely, from  [(x,p)] , the proposal is the path measure of





 [\displaystyle\mathrm{d}X_{t}] 
 [\displaystyle=P_{t}\,\mathrm{d}t\,,]
